\subsection{The coherent Kraus source of the twenty-four-outcome metric instrument}

Let $u_1,\ldots,u_6\in\R^3$ be antipodal icosahedral line representatives,
normalized so that
\[
 \sum_{a=1}^6u_au_a^{\mathsf T}=2I_3,
 \qquad |u_a\cdot u_b|^2=\frac15\quad(a\ne b).
\]
On the external Clifford factor put $A_i=\gamma^0\gamma^i$,
$A(u)=u_iA_i$, and $\tau_4=\tfrac14\Tr$.  Then
$A(u)^*=A(u)$, $A(u)^2=I$, and
$\tau_4(A(u)A(v))=u\cdot v$.  With two depth labels $d\in\Ztwo$ and
$\sigma\in\{\pm1\}$ define
\[
 K_{d,a,\sigma}
 =\frac1{\sqrt{12}}\frac{I+\sigma A(u_a)}2.
\]
The twenty-four branches are complete because
$\sum_{d,a,\sigma}K_{d,a,\sigma}^*K_{d,a,\sigma}=I$.
Let $\mathcal E_{24}=\C^{24}$ with basis $|d,a,\sigma\rangle$, define the Kraus
synthesis $\mathsf R|d,a,\sigma\rangle=K_{d,a,\sigma}$, and put
$\Gamma=\mathsf R^*\mathsf R$.

\begin{theorem}[Exact coherent source compression]
\label{thm:flagship-kraus-source-master}
There are orthogonal projections $P_0$ and $P_V$ of ranks one and three such
that
\[
 \boxed{\Gamma=\frac12P_0+\frac16P_V,\qquad\rank\Gamma=4.}
\]
Under the full icosahedral frame group, the coherent one-Read support is
\[
 \boxed{\Ran\Gamma\cong\mathbf1_+\oplus\mathbf3_-.}
\]
The exact matrix entries and the completely dephased pointer state are
\[
 \boxed{
 \langle d,a,\sigma|\Gamma|d',b,\tau\rangle
 =\frac{1+\sigma\tau\,u_a\cdot u_b}{48},
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Diag}(\Gamma)=\frac1{24}I_{24}.}
\]
Thus the coherent Kraus source has rank four, while the same instrument after a
complete fine-outcome Read has classical pointer rank twenty four.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let
\[
 j_0=\frac1{\sqrt{24}}\sum_{d,a,\sigma}|d,a,\sigma\rangle
\]
and define the isometry
\[
 J_Vx=\frac1{\sqrt8}\sum_{d,a,\sigma}
       \sigma\langle u_a,x\rangle|d,a,\sigma\rangle.
\]
The tight-frame identity gives $J_V^*J_V=I_3$, and $j_0\perp\Ran J_V$.
Direct summation gives
\[
 \mathsf Rj_0=\frac1{\sqrt2}I,
 \qquad
 \mathsf RJ_Vx=\frac1{\sqrt6}A(x).
\]
The four images are nonzero and Hilbert--Schmidt orthogonal.  Every Kraus
operator belongs to $\Span\{I,A_1,A_2,A_3\}$, so there can be no fifth singular
direction.  Squaring the two singular values proves the spectral formula and
rank.  The scalar vector is frame even, while $J_V\C^3$ is the ordinary
three-dimensional determinant-odd response representation.  Finally,
\[
 \tau_4(K_{d,a,\sigma}^*K_{d',b,\tau})
 =\frac1{48}\tau_4((I+\sigma A(u_a))(I+\tau A(u_b)))
\]
gives the entry formula.  Its diagonal is $1/24$, and diagonal projection
removes every off-diagonal entry.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Unique native determinant source and exact weight]
\label{cor:native-determinant-master}
The determinant character occurs with multiplicity one in
$(\Ran\Gamma)^{\otimes3}$.  If $e_{\rm vol}$ is its normalized exterior vector,
then
\[
 \boxed{\Gamma^{\otimes3}e_{\rm vol}=\frac1{216}e_{\rm vol}.}
\]
With the normalized sign-contrast convention of the coherent exterior Read,
which contributes a factor $2$ on each of the three sign-odd legs,
\[
 \boxed{M_{\rm ext}e_{\rm vol}=\frac1{27}e_{\rm vol}.}
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The determinant character is odd, so a determinant line in
$(\mathbf1_+\oplus\mathbf3_-)^{\otimes3}$ must lie in
$\mathbf3_-^{\otimes3}$.  On the rotational subgroup $A_5$, the standard
character has values $3,-1,0,\phi,1-\phi$ on classes of sizes
$1,15,20,12,12$.  Hence the invariant multiplicity is
\[
 \frac1{60}\left(27-15+12(\phi^3+(1-\phi)^3)\right)=1.
\]
The alternating volume tensor spans this line.  Since $\Gamma$ acts by $1/6$
on each ordinary response factor, its cube acts by $1/216$.  Normalizing each
sign-contrast leg multiplies the quadratic return by $2^3$, giving $1/27$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exact chronology tomography}

\begin{theorem}[Pointer populations determine a Schur chronology, not its coherences]
\label{thm:pointer-chronology-master}
Let $\mathcal D:M_{24}(\C)\to M_{24}(\C)$ be completely positive and
trace preserving and suppose that every fine pointer projection
$E_x=|x\rangle\langle x|$ is fixed.  Then there is a unique correlation matrix
\[
 C\succeq0,\qquad C_{xx}=1,
\]
such that
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal D(X)=C\circ X.}
\]
For a permutation symmetry $U_g$, the channel is covariant exactly when
$C_{gx,gy}=C_{xy}$.  Applied to the native source,
$\rho_C=C\circ\Gamma$ has the same diagonal $I_{24}/24$ for every $C$.
Whenever $\Gamma_{xy}\ne0$, the visibility is recovered by the finite
interference ratio
\[
 \boxed{C_{xy}=\frac{(\rho_C)_{xy}}{\Gamma_{xy}}.}
\]
Consequently the system channel and all endpoint populations fail to identify
the retained chronology, whereas pair-interference Reads identify every
coherence carried by the source.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $W$ be a Stinespring isometry for $\mathcal D$.  Since the output of the
pure state $E_x$ is again $E_x$, every purification has the form
$W|x\rangle=|x\rangle\otimes|\eta_x\rangle$ for a unit vector $\eta_x$.
Therefore
\[
 \mathcal D(|x\rangle\langle y|)
 =\langle\eta_y,\eta_x\rangle|x\rangle\langle y|.
\]
The coefficients form the Gram matrix $C$ and hence are positive semidefinite
with unit diagonal.  Conversely every such Gram matrix defines the displayed
isometry and Schur channel.  Covariance follows by applying both sides to
matrix units.  The remaining claims are immediate from
$\rho_C=C\circ\Gamma$ and $C_{xx}=1$; the real and imaginary parts of
$(\rho_C)_{xy}$ are expectations of
$E_{xy}+E_{yx}$ and $i(E_{yx}-E_{xy})$.
\end{proof}

Put $\upsilon=1/\sqrt5$.  In the real frame-covariant, depth-exchange-symmetric
class, let $c_{\delta,r}$ be the chronology visibility for depth difference
$\delta\in\{0,1\}$ and oriented-line inner product $r$.  The antipodal
coefficient is inactive because the native Gram entry vanishes there.  Define
\[
 \alpha_0=\frac1{24},\qquad
 \alpha_1=\frac{c_{1,1}}{24},
\]
\[
 \beta_\delta=\frac{1+\upsilon}{48}c_{\delta,+\upsilon},
 \qquad
 \gamma_\delta=\frac{1-\upsilon}{48}c_{\delta,-\upsilon}.
\]
Thus five off-diagonal visibilities are active.

\begin{theorem}[Structured chronology spectrum and determinant multiplicity]
\label{thm:structured-determinant-source-master}
For depth parity $s\in\{\pm1\}$ and
$\lambda\in\{\mathbf1,\mathbf5,\mathbf3,\mathbf3'\}$, put
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
\lambda&\mathbf1&\mathbf5&\mathbf3&\mathbf3'\\ \hline
\dim\lambda&1&5&3&3\\
p_\lambda&1&1&-1&-1\\
a_\lambda&5&-1&\sqrt5&-\sqrt5.
\end{array}
\]
The exact one-Read eigenvalues are
\[
 \boxed{
 \Lambda_{s,\lambda}
 =\alpha_0+s\alpha_1
  +a_\lambda(\beta_0+s\beta_1)
  +p_\lambda a_\lambda(\gamma_0+s\gamma_1),}
\]
with multiplicity $\dim\lambda$.  Positivity is equivalent to the eight
inequalities $\Lambda_{s,\lambda}\ge0$.

More generally, every frame-equivariant one-Read state has the block form
\[
 \rho\cong
 I_{\mathbf1}\otimes A_{\mathbf1}
 \oplus I_{\mathbf5}\otimes A_{\mathbf5}
 \oplus I_{\mathbf3}\otimes A_{\mathbf3}
 \oplus I_{\mathbf3'}\otimes A_{\mathbf3'},
 \qquad A_\lambda\in M_2(\C),\ A_\lambda\succeq0.
\]
Let $m_\lambda=\rank A_\lambda$.  The determinant-character multiplicity in
the three-Read source is
\[
 \boxed{
 r_{\det}
 =m_{\mathbf3}^{\,3}+m_{\mathbf3'}^{\,3}
 +3(m_{\mathbf3}+m_{\mathbf3'})m_{\mathbf5}^{\,2}.}
\]
It gives respectively $1,2,8,$ and $64$ for the native coherent source, the
complete sign-odd source, the full oriented-line source, and the completely
Read depth--line--sign pointer source.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The frame action on the twelve oriented icosahedral vertices is the commutative
association scheme generated by the identity, antipodal map, and the two
relations $v\cdot w=\pm\upsilon$.  Its permutation module is
$\mathbf1_+\oplus\mathbf5_+\oplus\mathbf3_-\oplus\mathbf3'_-$.
The depth Hadamard transform gives the parity $s$, the antipodal map has
eigenvalue $p_\lambda$, and the positive-neighbour adjacency has eigenvalues
$a_\lambda$.  Substitution gives the displayed spectrum and the positivity
criterion.

For a general equivariant state, Schur's lemma leaves the identity on each
irreducible carrier and an arbitrary positive matrix on its two-dimensional
depth multiplicity space.  The determinant character is odd.  A character
calculation for the icosahedral group gives one determinant copy in each of
$\mathbf3^{\otimes3}$ and $(\mathbf3')^{\otimes3}$, one in
$\mathbf3\otimes\mathbf5\otimes\mathbf5$ and one in
$\mathbf3'\otimes\mathbf5\otimes\mathbf5$, and none in the other parity-allowed
triple types.  Multiplicity spaces tensor independently, and the odd factor may
occupy any of the three positions in the mixed terms.  This yields the
polynomial.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Source rank, future leakage, and regulator stability}

Let $k$ be the normalized native depth vector in the $\mathbf3_-$ multiplicity
space.  For the corresponding $2\times2$ block define
\[
 \alpha=\langle k,A_{\mathbf3}k\rangle,
 \qquad
 \beta=\langle k,A_{\mathbf3}^2k\rangle,
 \qquad
 \nu=\beta-\alpha^2.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact canonical chronology leakage]
\label{thm:canonical-chronology-leakage-master}
For the three-Read tensor-product chronology and the canonical exterior vector,
\[
 \boxed{m_1=\alpha^3,\qquad m_2=\beta^3,}
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{
 m_2-m_1^2
 =3\alpha^4\nu+3\alpha^2\nu^2+\nu^3,\qquad
 \nu=\|(I-|k\rangle\langle k|)A_{\mathbf3}k\|^2.}
\]
Equivalently, if $P_{\rm K}^{(3)}$ is the native coherent three-Read support,
$Z_{\rm K}=2P_{\rm K}^{(3)}-I$, and $M$ is an equivariant self-adjoint completed
return, then
\[
 \boxed{
 \|Me_{\rm vol}\|^2-|\langle e_{\rm vol},Me_{\rm vol}\rangle|^2
 =\|(I-P_{\rm K}^{(3)})Me_{\rm vol}\|^2
 =\frac14\|[M,Z_{\rm K}]e_{\rm vol}\|^2.}
\]
Thus an enlarged horizon-zero determinant source and leakage from the selected
canonical line are distinct notions.  In particular, depth-exchange symmetry
may increase $r_{\det}$ while keeping $\nu=0$ and the canonical line invariant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The exterior vector uses three ordinary $\mathbf3_-$ factors with common depth
vector $k$.  The first and second tensor-product moments factor, giving
$m_1=\alpha^3$ and $m_2=\beta^3$.  Since
$\beta=\alpha^2+\nu$, expansion of $\beta^3-\alpha^6$ gives the polynomial.
For the commutator identity, equivariance and uniqueness of the determinant
line inside $P_{\rm K}^{(3)}$ imply
$P_{\rm K}^{(3)}Me_{\rm vol}\in\C e_{\rm vol}$.  Orthogonal decomposition gives
the variance.  Since $Z_{\rm K}e_{\rm vol}=e_{\rm vol}$,
$[M,Z_{\rm K}]e_{\rm vol}=2(I-P_{\rm K}^{(3)})Me_{\rm vol}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Structured source frame criterion]
\label{cor:structured-frame-master}
For a regulator family of equivariant chronology blocks $A_{\lambda,\epsilon}$,
assume that every intended surviving block has its least positive eigenvalue
bounded below by one constant $c_*>0$.  Then the intended one-Read source frame
has lower bound $c_*$ and its three-Read tensor source has lower bound $c_*^3$.
If an intended positive eigenvalue tends to zero, its normalized eigenvector is
an explicit collapsing source direction, so saturation need not survive the
limit.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The equivariant decomposition is orthogonal and the source quadratic form on
each block is bounded below by its least positive eigenvalue.  Tensoring three
such inequalities gives $c_*^3$.  Conversely an eigenvector whose eigenvalue
tends to zero has unit norm but source norm tending to zero.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Joint clock--geometry source audit and source-native ADM closure}

Let
\[
 \mathcal V_{\rm met}=\C u\oplus V_5
\]
be the homogeneous-plus-shape metric-control module, and let
\[
 S_{\rm clk},S_{\rm geo}:\mathcal V_{\rm met}\to\mathcal H_{\rm phys}
\]
be the actual retained semantic-clock and determinant/curvature source maps in
one null-completed physical quotient with one positive future transfer $T$.
Put
\[
 A=S_{\rm clk}^*S_{\rm clk},\qquad
 B=S_{\rm clk}^*S_{\rm geo},\qquad
 D=S_{\rm geo}^*S_{\rm geo}.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact clock--geometry action audit]
\label{thm:ADM-action-audit-master}
The geometry source lying outside the clock-source range has Gram
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathbb G_{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}}
 =D-B^*A^\dagger B
 =S_{\rm geo}^*(I-P_{\rm clk})S_{\rm geo}\succeq0,}
\]
where $P_{\rm clk}=S_{\rm clk}A^\dagger S_{\rm clk}^*$.  Moreover,
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathbb G_{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}}=0
 \iff
 \Ran S_{\rm geo}\subseteq\Ran S_{\rm clk},}
\]
and
\[
 \rank\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\B^*&D\end{pmatrix}-\rank A
 =\rank\mathbb G_{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}}.
\]
When $A,D\succ0$, the normalized cross operator
$\mathcal C_{\rm cg}=A^{-1/2}BD^{-1/2}$ is a contraction and
\[
 \boxed{
 D^{-1/2}\mathbb G_{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}}D^{-1/2}
 =I-\mathcal C_{\rm cg}^*\mathcal C_{\rm cg}.}
\]
Its singular values are the principal cosines of the two physical source
ranges.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$P_{\rm clk}$ is the standard Gram formula for the orthogonal projection onto
$\Ran S_{\rm clk}$.  Hence
$B^*A^\dagger B=S_{\rm geo}^*P_{\rm clk}S_{\rm geo}$, proving the Schur
identity and positivity.  Vanishing is equivalent to
$(I-P_{\rm clk})S_{\rm geo}=0$.  The rank increment equals the dimension of
this orthogonal component.  Finally,
$S_{\rm clk}A^{-1/2}$ and $S_{\rm geo}D^{-1/2}$ are isometries; their cross
product is $\mathcal C_{\rm cg}$, which proves the contraction, residual, and
principal-angle claims.
\end{proof}

Let $P_0$ and $P_5$ be the projections of
$\mathcal V_{\rm met}=\C u\oplus V_5$ onto the homogeneous and shape
summands.  Suppose the independently computed primitive frame group $\Gamma$
acts by the multiplicity-free representation
$\pi=\mathbf1\oplus\pi_5$, and that for one physical action $U_g$ on
$\mathcal H_{\rm phys}$,
\begin{equation}
  U_gS_a=S_a\pi(g),
  \qquad a\in\{{\rm clk},{\rm geo}\},
  \qquad [T,U_g]=0.
  \label{eq:cross-source-covariance-master}
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Multiplicity-free relative-source reduction]
\label{thm:relative-source-sector-master}
Under \eqref{eq:cross-source-covariance-master},
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
  A=a_0P_0+a_5P_5,
  \qquad
  B=b_0P_0+b_5P_5,
  \qquad
  D=d_0P_0+d_5P_5,}
  \label{eq:relative-source-blocks-master}
\end{equation}
where $a_\alpha,d_\alpha\ge0$ and $b_\alpha\in\C$.  In formulas containing
$a_\alpha^{-1}$, the Moore--Penrose convention is used; the amplitude/phase
statements below are restricted to sectors with $a_\alpha,d_\alpha>0$.  On
every loaded sector,
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{|b_\alpha|^2\le a_\alpha d_\alpha,
  \qquad \alpha\in\{0,5\}.}
  \label{eq:relative-source-CS-master}
\end{equation}
The geometry-outside-clock residual is
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
  \mathbb G_{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}}
  =\left(d_0-\frac{|b_0|^2}{a_0}\right)P_0
   +\left(d_5-\frac{|b_5|^2}{a_5}\right)P_5.}
  \label{eq:relative-source-residual-master}
\end{equation}
If this residual vanishes and $a_\alpha,d_\alpha>0$, then
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
  S_{\rm geo}P_\alpha
  =\lambda_\alpha e^{i\phi_\alpha}S_{\rm clk}P_\alpha,}
  \qquad
  \lambda_\alpha=\sqrt{d_\alpha/a_\alpha},
  \qquad
  e^{i\phi_\alpha}=b_\alpha/|b_\alpha|.
  \label{eq:relative-source-phase-master}
\end{equation}
Thus primitive covariance reduces the entire same-range discrepancy to two
amplitudes and two phases, but does not fix them.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The operators $A,B,D$ commute with the multiplicity-free representation
$\mathbf1\oplus\pi_5$.  Schur's lemma gives
\eqref{eq:relative-source-blocks-master}.  Positivity of the two-by-two scalar
compression of the joint Gram gives
\eqref{eq:relative-source-CS-master}.  Substitution in
\Cref{thm:ADM-action-audit-master} gives the residual formula.  Equality in the
sectorwise Cauchy--Schwarz inequality makes the normalized source isometries
collinear, giving the amplitude and phase formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite clock--geometry source-identity defects]
\label{def:source-identity-defects-master}
Assume $a_\alpha,d_\alpha>0$ on the two loaded sectors and put
\[
  \lambda_\alpha=\sqrt{d_\alpha/a_\alpha},
  \qquad
  \zeta_\alpha=
  \begin{cases}
    b_\alpha/|b_\alpha|,&b_\alpha\ne0,\\
    1,&b_\alpha=0.
  \end{cases}
\]
Define
\begin{align}
  \delta_\perp^2
  &:=\Tr\mathbb G_{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}},
  \label{eq:source-defect-perp-master}\\
  \delta_{\rm amp}^2
  &:=(\lambda_0-1)^2+5(\lambda_5-1)^2,
  \label{eq:source-defect-amp-master}\\
  \delta_{\rm ph}^2
  &:=|\zeta_0-1|^2+5|\zeta_5-1|^2.
  \label{eq:source-defect-phase-master}
\end{align}
Then
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{
  S_{\rm geo}=S_{\rm clk}
  \iff
  \delta_\perp=\delta_{\rm amp}=\delta_{\rm ph}=0.}
  \label{eq:source-defect-equality-master}
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite clock--geometry branch-interference tomography]
\label{thm:cross-source-tomography-master}
Use the convention that the Hilbert-space inner product is conjugate-linear
in its first variable.  Suppose the physical process admits coherent branch
preparations of $S_{\rm clk}v$ and $S_{\rm geo}w$ with relative phases
$1,-1,i,-i$.  Then
\begin{align}
  \Re\langle S_{\rm clk}v,S_{\rm geo}w\rangle
  &=\frac14\left(
    \norm{S_{\rm clk}v+S_{\rm geo}w}^2
   -\norm{S_{\rm clk}v-S_{\rm geo}w}^2
  \right),
  \label{eq:cross-source-real-master}\\
  \Im\langle S_{\rm clk}v,S_{\rm geo}w\rangle
  &=\frac14\left(
    \norm{S_{\rm clk}v-iS_{\rm geo}w}^2
   -\norm{S_{\rm clk}v+iS_{\rm geo}w}^2
  \right).
  \label{eq:cross-source-imag-master}
\end{align}
A finite basis of $\mathcal V_{\rm met}$ therefore reconstructs the complete
cross Gram $B=S_{\rm clk}^*S_{\rm geo}$ and every defect in
\Cref{def:source-identity-defects-master}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
These are the real and imaginary complex polarization identities.  Applying
them to basis vectors reconstructs every matrix element of $B$.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[Amplitude-level comparison is independent physical data]
\label{fire:cross-source-nonidentifiability-master}
The predictive Radon--Nikodym theorem canonically identifies the positive
effects of the two CP refinements.  It does not by itself identify the relative
Stinespring partial isometry of two amplitude-level branches.  If both branches
are physically fixed by the same canonical positive-root realization, equal
effects do identify their amplitudes.  Otherwise
\Cref{thm:cross-source-tomography-master}, or another admitted coherent bridge,
is independent information.  If no such bridge is physical, same-history
source identity is nonidentifiable even when every marginal response agrees.
\end{firewall}


\begin{theorem}[Cross-Hankel chronology theorem]
\label{thm:clock-geometry-cross-Hankel-master}
For $a\in\{{\rm clk},{\rm geo}\}$ define the depth-$r$ history synthesis
\[
 \mathcal V_r^a(x_0,\ldots,x_r)
 :=\sum_{j=0}^{r}T^jS_ax_j,
\]
and for $a,b\in\{{\rm clk},{\rm geo}\}$ put
\[
 \mathbb H_r^{ab}
 =(\mathcal V_r^a)^*\mathcal V_r^b
 =\bigl[S_a^*T^{i+j}S_b\bigr]_{0\le i,j\le r}.
\]
Let $\Pi_r^{\rm clk}$ project onto
$\Span\{T^j\Ran S_{\rm clk}:0\le j\le r\}$.  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathbb G_r^{{\rm geo}\mid{\rm clk}}
 ={}&\mathbb H_r^{{\rm geo},{\rm geo}}
 -(\mathbb H_r^{{\rm clk},{\rm geo}})^*
  (\mathbb H_r^{{\rm clk},{\rm clk}})^\dagger
  \mathbb H_r^{{\rm clk},{\rm geo}}\\
 ={}&(\mathcal V_r^{\rm geo})^*
 (I-\Pi_r^{\rm clk})\mathcal V_r^{\rm geo}\succeq0.
 \end{aligned}}
\]
Its rank is the number of additional geometry-history directions through depth
$r$.  If the first vanishing occurs at depth $d$, then
\[
 \boxed{S_{\rm geo}=\sum_{n=0}^{d}T^nS_{\rm clk}R_n}
\]
with minimum-norm coefficients reconstructed by the Moore--Penrose inverse of
$\mathbb H_d^{{\rm clk},{\rm clk}}$.  The minimum such $d$ is the physical
chronology degree.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The block matrix of the joint clock and geometry history synthesis is a Gram
matrix.  Taking its generalized Schur complement gives the Gram of the
geometry histories orthogonal to the clock packet, exactly as in the first
formula.  Vanishing is equivalent to range inclusion.  Solving the inclusion
by least squares gives the displayed coefficients; minimality of $d$ is by
definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Primitive-frame chronology polynomials]
\label{cor:clock-geometry-chronology-polynomial-master}
Assume in addition that $T$ is primitive-frame covariant.  The coefficient maps
in
\[
  S_{\rm geo}=\sum_{n=0}^{d}T^nS_{\rm clk}R_n
\]
may be chosen in the commutant of $\mathbf1\oplus\pi_5$:
\[
  R_n=r_{n,0}P_0+r_{n,5}P_5.
\]
Thus the homogeneous and shape sectors have explicit chronology polynomials
\[
  \boxed{
  q_\alpha(z)=\sum_{n=0}^{d}r_{n,\alpha}z^n,
  \qquad \alpha\in\{0,5\}.}
\]
The constant polynomial one is exact same-history identity.  A nonconstant
polynomial is a measurable delay or future filter and cannot be renamed as a
local common-action coefficient.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Average any coefficient solution over the primitive frame group.  Covariance of
$T,S_{\rm clk},S_{\rm geo}$ preserves the source identity.  The averaged
coefficients commute with the multiplicity-free frame representation and hence
have the stated form by Schur's lemma.
\end{proof}



\begin{countertheorem}[Equal marginal responses do not prove common action]
\label{cth:equal-Hessian-not-source-master}
There are source maps with
$S_{\rm clk}^*S_{\rm clk}=S_{\rm geo}^*S_{\rm geo}=I$ and identical future
moments at every depth, while their physical ranges are orthogonal and
$B=0$.  Therefore equality of every marginal Hessian or moment does not imply
one retained same-history action.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Take any realization $(T_0,S_0)$ and form
$T=T_0\oplus T_0$,
$S_{\rm clk}=S_0\oplus0$,
$S_{\rm geo}=0\oplus S_0$.  The marginal moments are identical, while the
cross Gram is zero and the source Schur residual equals the full geometry Gram.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite source-cyclic realization equivalence]
\label{thm:source-cyclic-equivalence-master}
Let $(T_1,S_1)$ and $(T_2,S_2)$ be finite positive-contraction source
realizations, possibly on different Hilbert spaces.  Suppose
\begin{equation}
  S_1^*T_1^nS_1=S_2^*T_2^nS_2
  \qquad(0\le n\le2r+1),
  \label{eq:source-cyclic-moment-equality-master}
\end{equation}
and both source Krylov packets stabilize at depth $r$.  Then there is a unique
unitary between the stabilized source-cyclic packets satisfying
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{US_1=S_2,\qquad UT_1=T_2U.}
  \label{eq:source-cyclic-unitary-master}
\end{equation}
Thus equality of sufficiently many flat moments proves canonical abstract
unitary equivalence, but not physical identity of two branches already embedded
in one larger process.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On finite history sums define
\[
  U\left(\sum_{n=0}^{r}T_1^nS_1x_n\right)
  :=\sum_{n=0}^{r}T_2^nS_2x_n.
\]
The moment identities make the two block Hankel Grams equal, so the map is
well defined and isometric.  Flatness makes both packets invariant under their
transfers, giving the intertwining relation.  It is onto by construction and
unique because the source histories span.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Operational clock--geometry source alternative]
\label{thm:clock-geometry-alternative-master}
Apply the finite tests in the following order.  Exactly one terminal outcome is
returned.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(A\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item If neither a coherent branch-interference port nor a common canonical
      positive-root realization is physical, source identity is
      nonidentifiable and no common-action claim is permitted.
\item If the zero-depth residual is nonzero and no finite cross-Hankel depth
      contains the geometry source, its residual range is an explicit additional
      action source.
\item If the zero-depth residual is nonzero but the first cross-Hankel closure
      occurs at finite depth $d>0$, the reconstructed polynomial
      $\sum_{n=0}^{d}T^nS_{\rm clk}R_n$ is a measurable chronology filter and not
      a local common-action coefficient.
\item If the residual vanishes but one of the two sector amplitudes or phases is
      nontrivial, the corresponding homogeneous or spin-two split is retained.
\item If the source defects vanish but the score instrument fails the
      persistent-transfer certificate, the frequency-mixing test, or
      \Cref{thm:semantic-clock-depth-two-master}, the corresponding score-memory,
      frequency-changing, intra-frequency, or central semantic split is retained.
\item Only when all preceding tests pass does exact physical source identity hold
      and \Cref{thm:Clifford-common-action-master} become applicable.
\end{enumerate}
No nonzero residual is absorbed into $\chi$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The amplitude-access test is the firewall above.  With cross data, the
zero-depth Schur residual is either positive or zero.  In the positive branch,
\Cref{thm:clock-geometry-cross-Hankel-master} returns either no finite closure
or a first finite chronology degree.  In the zero branch,
\Cref{thm:relative-source-sector-master} returns either nontrivial sector
amplitude/phase data or exact source equality.  The independent semantic-depth
test then gives the last split or the common-action branch.
\end{proof}


\begin{theorem}[Source-native clock--geometry common-action theorem]
\label{thm:Clifford-common-action-master}
Assume:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(A\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item the two source maps are actual retained histories in one null-completed
      quotient and use one physical transfer $T(q)$;
\item their Grams are nonsingular on $\mathbf1\oplus V_5$;
\item a coherent branch-interference port or one common canonical positive-root
      realization is physically admitted;
\item the admitted amplitude-level bridge and independent absolute normalization
      give $\delta_\perp=\delta_{\rm amp}=\delta_{\rm ph}=0$;
\item the score instrument passes the persistent-transfer, frequency-mixing,
      and depth-two common-clock tests of
      \Cref{def:persistent-score-transfer-master,thm:score-frequency-mixing-master,thm:semantic-clock-depth-two-master};
\item the determinant-dual, physical connection, face-composition,
      deformation, and continuum certificates pass.
\end{enumerate}
Then
\[
 \boxed{S_{\rm geo}=S_{\rm clk}},
\]
all future moments and analytic control derivatives agree, and one physical
spin-two coefficient $\chi$ multiplies both the kinetic parent and curvature
Read.  Hence
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal H_{\rm g}
 =\frac{\chi^{-1}}{\sqrt q}
  \left(\pi^{ab}\pi_{ab}-\frac12\pi^2\right)
 -\chi\sqrt q\,R+\Lambda_H\sqrt q,}
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{c=\frac12,\qquad a=\chi^{-1},\qquad b=\chi,\qquad ab=1.}
\]
If any test fails, the corresponding nonidentifiability certificate, Schur
residual, chronology polynomial, sector amplitude/phase, or semantic loading is
retained explicitly; it is not absorbed into $\chi$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The vanishing source residual and the sector reduction of
\Cref{thm:relative-source-sector-master} give
$S_{\rm geo}=S_{\rm clk}R$, with one amplitude and phase on each of
$\mathbf1$ and $V_5$.  Vanishing of the three measured defects makes $R=I$,
proving physical source identity.  Using the same source and transfer gives
equality of every future moment and, by differentiation, every analytic
response.  The persistent-transfer, frequency-mixing, and depth-two tests
remove hidden instrument memory, intra-frequency multiplicity, and marked
central score loading.  The
spin-two coefficient is therefore one number $\chi$.  The determinant Hessian
in \Cref{thm:spectral-source-Ward-master} gives $c=1/2$, and the Legendre
transform of a Lagrangian kinetic coefficient $\chi$ gives the Hamiltonian
coefficient $\chi^{-1}$ while the curvature descendant retains $\chi$.
The remaining hypotheses are precisely those required to interpret the finite
curvature and bracket as the local ADM generator.
\end{proof}

% =============================================================================
\section{Physical connection and finite deformation algebra}
\label{sec:physical-connection-deformation}
% =============================================================================

Let $W_x:V_x\to\mathcal H_x$ be the reconstructed three-dimensional writer at
cell $x$, with $G_x=W_x^*W_x\succ0$.  For an oriented edge $e:x\to y$, let
$\Phi_e$ be the actual shortened two-cell history and put
\[
 C_e=W_y^*\Phi_eW_x,\qquad
 L_e=G_y^{-1}C_e,\qquad
 R_e=(I-P_y)\Phi_eW_x,
\]
where $P_y=W_yG_y^{-1}W_y^*$.

\begin{theorem}[Physical connection decision]
\label{thm:physical-connection-master}
The exact link decomposition is
\[
 \boxed{\Phi_eW_x=W_yL_e+R_e,\qquad
 R_e^*R_e=W_x^*\Phi_e^*\Phi_eW_x-C_e^*G_y^{-1}C_e\succeq0.}
\]
Thus $R_e=0$ is the finite frame-closure certificate.  If the physical link is
a unital CP Clifford map closing on grade one, then
\[
 L_e^*G_yL_e\preceq G_x;
\]
equality holds exactly when the writer Clifford generators lie in its
multiplicative domain.  Every invertible closed link has the unique metric polar
factorization
\[
 \boxed{L_e=U_e\mathsf C_e,}
\]
where $U_e$ is a $G_x$--$G_y$ isometry and $\mathsf C_e$ is positive and
$G_x$-self-adjoint.  The data fall into exactly one of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(L\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item Levi--Civita: link closure, metricity, orientability, zero discrete
      torsion, and same-history face composition pass;
\item Riemann--Cartan: metricity and face composition pass but torsion survives;
\item metric-affine: the polar stretch or discrete nonmetricity is nonzero;
\item no closed physical connection: writer leakage, rank loss, orientation
      obstruction, or face residual survives.
\end{enumerate}
Only in \textup{(L1)} does the Clifford--Palatini Read converge to
$\sqrt q\,R(q)$ without an additional contorsion or disformation source.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first formula is orthogonal least squares onto $\Ran W_y$; multiplying by
its adjoint gives the residual Gram.  Kadison--Schwarz applied to the grade-one
Clifford images gives the contraction inequality.  Equality in
Kadison--Schwarz is the multiplicative-domain criterion and preserves the
Clifford anticommutator, hence the metric.  The metric polar decomposition is
the ordinary polar decomposition of
$G_y^{1/2}L_eG_x^{-1/2}$.  Discrete metricity and torsion are respectively the
symmetric metric defect and the antisymmetrized covariant coframe difference.
Their smooth limits are the continuum nonmetricity and torsion tensors.  The
actual face amplitude is compared with the ordered product of the actual link
histories; a nonzero difference is an independent source.  The fundamental
theorem of Riemannian geometry gives Levi--Civita uniqueness exactly in case
\textup{(L1)}.  In the other cases the standard decompositions
$\omega=\omega^{\rm LC}+K$ and
$\Gamma=\Gamma^{\rm LC}+K+L$ retain contorsion or disformation explicitly.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact five-term deformation anomaly]
\label{thm:deformation-anomaly-master}
Let $P_h$ be the null-completed physical projection, let
$\mathcal C_h^{\rm full}[N,M]$ be the full primitive normal commutator, and let
$\mathcal D_h^{\rm phys}$ be the predeclared physical tangential-history
synthesis.  Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &\mathfrak J_h\mathcal C_h^{\rm full}[N,M]
 -D\!\left[q^\sharp(N\,\dd M-M\,\dd N)\right]\\
 &\quad=\mathfrak J_h\mathcal A_{\rm source}^{(h)}[N,M]
 +\mathfrak J_h\mathcal A_{\rm normal}^{(h)}[N,M]
 +\mathfrak J_h\mathcal A_{\rm connection}^{(h)}[N,M]\\
 &\qquad
 +\mathfrak J_h\mathcal A_{\rm interface}^{(h)}[N,M]
 +\mathcal A_{\rm scaling}^{(h)}[N,M].
 \end{aligned}}
\]
Here the source term is the orthogonal residual outside the tangential-history
span; the normal term is the difference between the observed structure map and
the metric-predicted map; the connection term is the difference between the
physical and Levi--Civita tangential generators; the interface term is
\[
 \boxed{-i\bigl(P_hAQ_hBP_h-P_hBQ_hAP_h\bigr)}
\]
for the two normal histories $A,B$; and the scaling term is the remaining
finite-to-continuum reconstruction defect.  The target hypersurface-deformation
bracket follows if and only if these five terms vanish in the stated topology.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Add and subtract, in order: the separately shortened commutator, its projection
onto the predeclared tangential source, the metric-predicted finite structure
map, the Levi--Civita tangential realization, and the continuum tangential
generator.  This gives the displayed telescoping identity.  Expanding
$P_h[A,B]P_h$ with $I=P_h+Q_h$ gives the interface term exactly.  Since each
other term is defined as one consecutive difference in the telescope, no
cancellation or covariance assumption is hidden in the identity.
\end{proof}

\section{Independent continuum clauses, matter, and the one-sequence Einstein handoff}

\label{sec:Einstein-extension}

\begin{principle}[Decomposed continuum completion]
\label{prin:ESSC}
For one concrete regulator sequence distinguish the following four clauses.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(E\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item \emph{Exhaustion}: anchored energy-damped local screens exhaust the
      regulated future core uniformly in the ambient volume.
\item \emph{Scaling}: the interacting local field law has a nontrivial
      continuum limit with an independently certified non-Gaussian connected
      response.
\item \emph{Renormalization}: the renormalized generating functionals converge
      in $C^1_{\rm loc}$ on a source Banach space fixed before the limit.
\item \emph{Ward}: the microscopic symmetry and composite/current insertions
      converge with vanishing anomaly.
\end{enumerate}
No clause is defined by another.  A full continuum handoff requires all four
for the same regulator, together with the gravitational connection and
constraint certificates.
\end{principle}

\begin{theorem}[Continuum-clause independence and heat-bath firewall]
\label{thm:ESSC-independence-master}
The four clauses of \Cref{prin:ESSC} are logically independent.  There is an
explicit spatially interacting $SO(2)$ quartic path-Gibbs regulator with a
local reversible heat-bath future satisfying all four.  Nevertheless, under
ordinary Riemann smearing its bounded local heat-bath normal commutator tends to
zero too rapidly to yield a nonzero hypersurface-deformation bracket.  Hence
\[
 \boxed{\text{continuum ESSC future}\ne\text{gravitational normal constraint}}
\]
without an additional coherent normal-history theorem.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Finite-range dependency cones give exhaustion; Brownian-bridge interpolation
and the quartic density give the interacting scaling limit; source-dual
exponential integrability gives $C^1_{\rm loc}$ convergence; microscopic
$SO(2)$ changes of variables give the exact current Ward identity.  These
proofs use distinct estimates, and the standard nonlocal-bridge, oscillating
scaling, source-rescaling, and asymmetric-potential countermodels separate the
four implications.  For a bounded local normal piece $n_x^h$ with Riemann
weight $h^d$, edge count $O(h^{-d})$, lapse wedge $O(h)$, and reconstruction
growth $h^{-r}$, the commutator norm is $O(h^{d+1-r})$.  In $d=3$, a bounded or
first-difference reconstruction gives $O(h^4)$ or $O(h^3)$, so the nonzero HDA
target cannot be obtained from the heat-bath generator.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Moving-frame matter and universal relative coupling}

For a cell volume $\mu_x$, positive one-sided conductances $C_{x,e}$, canonical
matter pairs $(\Phi_x^A,p_{x,A})$, and a local potential $V$, define
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 h_{{\rm m},x}={}&\frac{p_x^2}{2\mu_x}+\mu_xV(\Phi_x)
 +\frac14\sum_{e=\{x,y\}\ni x}C_{x,e}|\Phi_y-\Phi_x|^2,\\
 H_{\rm m}^h[N]={}&\sum_xN_xh_{{\rm m},x}.
 \end{aligned}
\]
For an oriented edge $e=(x,y)$ put
\[
 \mathfrak d_{e,h}^{\rm m}
 =\frac14\sum_A
 \left\{
 \frac{C_{y,e}}{\mu_x}p_{x,A}
 +\frac{C_{x,e}}{\mu_y}p_{y,A},
 \Phi_y^A-\Phi_x^A
 \right\}.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact moving-frame matter bracket]
\label{thm:moving-frame-matter-master}
On the finite canonical core,
\[
 \boxed{
 -i[H_{\rm m}^h[N],H_{\rm m}^h[M]]
 =\sum_{e=(x,y)}(N_xM_y-M_xN_y)\mathfrak d_{e,h}^{\rm m}.}
\]
If the conductance second moment converges to
$\sqrt q\,q^{-1}$, the right-hand side converges to the matter diffeomorphism
constraint with shift $q^\sharp(N\,\dd M-M\,\dd N)$.  For typed sectors with
positive relative amplitudes $\lambda_r$, positivity of the species-current
Gram forces $\lambda_r=1$ whenever the total bracket closes on the unweighted
physical matter diffeomorphism.  Endpoint-local dependence on the canonical
metric register makes the antisymmetrized gravity--matter cross bracket vanish
exactly.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Kinetic terms commute with one another and all multiplication potentials commute.
The local-potential cross terms carry the symmetric factor $N_xM_x$ and cancel
in the antisymmetrization.  For each edge, the two endpoint
kinetic--gradient commutators combine into the displayed anticommutator.  A
Taylor expansion of $\Phi_y-\Phi_x$ and the conductance second-moment identity
give the continuum diffeomorphism density.  For amplitudes $\lambda_r$, the
species currents appear with coefficients $\lambda_r^2$; a positive Gram makes
the representation unique, so positive amplitudes equal one.  Finally, the
metric derivative of an endpoint density is multiplied by the same local
lapse in the gravitational and matter terms, making the antisymmetrized cross
commutator cancel.
\end{proof}

\subsection{One-sequence compactness and the covariant action}

\begin{theorem}[One-sequence Einstein--matter handoff]
\label{thm:Einstein-extension-master}
Assume on one regulator sequence:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(C\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item the source-native ADM theorem \Cref{thm:Clifford-common-action-master};
\item the Levi--Civita and face-composition branch of
      \Cref{thm:physical-connection-master};
\item vanishing of the five deformation anomalies in
      \Cref{thm:deformation-anomaly-master};
\item the moving-frame matter bracket of
      \Cref{thm:moving-frame-matter-master} and vanishing matter interface and
      cross residuals;
\item a null-completed positive history Gram with uniform coarse and fast
      margins and bounded harmonic lift;
\item discrete space--time compactness, zero concentration measure, convergence
      of the first variations, and vanishing Legendre and boundary defects;
\item convergence $\chi_h\to\chi>0$ and
      $\Lambda_{H,h}\to\Lambda_H$.
\end{enumerate}
Then a subsequence converges to the covariant action
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 S[g,\Phi]={}&\chi\int\sqrt{-g}\,(R-2\Lambda_{\rm cov})\,\dd^4x\\
 &-\int\sqrt{-g}\left[
 \frac12g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\Phi^A\partial_\nu\Phi^A+V(\Phi)
 \right]\dd^4x,
 \qquad
 \Lambda_{\rm cov}=\frac{\Lambda_H}{2\chi}.
 \end{aligned}}
\]
Its Euler--Lagrange equations are
\[
 \boxed{
 2\chi(G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda_{\rm cov}g_{\mu\nu})=T_{\mu\nu},
 \qquad
 \nabla^\mu T_{\mu\nu}=0.}
\]
No Clausius premise is needed for the canonical-to-covariant implication once
the common action and compactness certificates have been proved.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The null-completed positive history Gram and its Schur margins give uniform
Sobolev control of the gauge-fixed metric, connection, matter field, and their
canonical momenta.  The discrete space--time translation estimates give
Rellich compactness.  Vanishing of the concentration measure upgrades the
weak convergence to the strength needed for every nonlinear energy.  The
finite stationarity equation therefore passes to the first variation of the
limiting canonical ADM-plus-matter action.  The vanishing Legendre defects give
\[
 \pi^{ab}=\chi\sqrt q(K^{ab}-q^{ab}K),
 \qquad
 p_A=\frac{\sqrt q}{N}(\dot\Phi_A-N^a\partial_a\Phi_A).
\]
Substitution and the Gauss--Codazzi identity yield the displayed covariant
action, modulo the controlled boundary term.  Metric variation gives the
Einstein equation and matter variation gives the matter equation.  Diffeomorphism
invariance gives the off-shell Noether identity
$\nabla^\mu\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}+\mathcal E_A\partial_\nu\Phi^A=0$;
on matter shell this is $\nabla^\mu T_{\mu\nu}=0$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Orientation selection is a separate phase theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Primitive deck-interaction phase alternative]
\label{thm:orientation-phase-master}
Let the primitive equal/unequal deck edge weights be $w_{=},w_{\ne}>0$ and put
\[
 K_{\rm deck}=\frac12\log\frac{w_=}{w_{\ne}}.
\]
On the cubic lattice:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(O\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item if $w_=w_{\ne}$, boundary conditions have exactly zero influence and no
      orientation is selected;
\item if $6\tanh|K_{\rm deck}|<1$, the infinite-volume state is unique,
      boundary influence decays exponentially, and spontaneous, boundary, and
      two-sector superselection selection are impossible;
\item if $K_{\rm deck}>0$ and
      $144e^{-2K_{\rm deck}}\le10^{-2}$, plus and minus boundary conditions
      produce distinct limiting states with opposite nonzero deck order;
\item at zero temperature and $K_{\rm deck}>0$, the two uniform ground states
      define disjoint factorial sectors exchanged by the deck involution;
\item detailed-balance selected phases have zero stationary entropy production,
      so orientation order alone is not an arrow of time.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $K=0$ the Gibbs law is a product and boundary variables do not enter any
interior conditional probability.  Changing one neighbour changes the local
binary conditional law by at most $\tanh|K|$; summing over six neighbours gives
the Dobrushin coefficient and the uniqueness/no-selection conclusion.  In the
ferromagnetic branch, flipping a finite droplet costs
$2K|\partial_E D|$.  Counting connected closed dual surfaces by
$400n^3 144^{n-1}$ makes the Peierls sum smaller than $0.031$ under the stated
condition, so the plus state has strictly positive magnetization and the minus
state its negative.  At zero temperature only the two uniform configurations
minimize the positive bond energy; their product-state GNS representations are
disjoint, separated by the macroscopic magnetization central sequence.
Detailed balance makes each probability current cancel its reverse, so the
entropy-production sum is zero.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Metrological coefficients}

\begin{theorem}[Independent Newton and cosmological metrology]
\label{thm:metrology-master}
On the physical spin-two tangent space let $\mathbb H_{2,h}$ be the measured
effective Hessian and $\mathbb K_{2,h}$ the unit-coefficient canonical
stiffness.  Put
\[
 \mathbb M_{\chi,h}
 =\mathbb K_{2,h}^{-1/2}\mathbb H_{2,h}\mathbb K_{2,h}^{-1/2},
 \qquad
 \widehat\chi_h=\frac1{d_{2,h}}\Tr\mathbb M_{\chi,h}.
\]
The response is one scalar stiffness exactly when
$\mathbb M_{\chi,h}-\widehat\chi_hI=0$.  If
$\widehat\chi_h\to\chi>0$ and the renewal length is calibrated as $\ell_*$,
then
\[
 \boxed{G_{\rm eff}=\frac{\ell_*^2}{16\pi\chi}.}
\]
For the homogeneous path $q(\sigma)=e^{2\sigma}q_0$, an unnormalized absolute
vacuum branch action $\Gamma_h^{\rm vac}$ gives
\[
 \boxed{
 \widehat\Lambda_{H,h}
 =\frac1{3\mathcal V_h}\frac{\dd}{\dd\sigma}
   \Gamma_h^{\rm vac}(0).}
\]
Normalized Store--Read probabilities cannot determine this coefficient: the
multiplication of every branch amplitude by $e^{-c(q)}$ leaves all normalized
states unchanged and shifts the absolute homogeneous score.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion is the scalarity criterion for the normalized spin-two
Hessian.  The ADM normalization convention
$\chi=\ell_*^2/(16\pi G_{\rm eff})$ gives the displayed conversion.  The
homogeneous variation of $\Lambda_H\sqrt q$ in three dimensions is
$3\Lambda_H\mathcal V_h$, giving the second formula.  The final invariance is
immediate because a common scalar factor cancels from every normalized branch
probability.
\end{proof}
