% =============================================================================
\section{The universal growing Grand Tensor}
\label{sec:universal-grand-tensor}
% =============================================================================

\begin{definition}[Cutoff-compatible common-source datum]
\label{def:grand-datum}
For each cutoff $X$, let $\mathfrak A_X$ be a finite set of typed sectors,
$\mathcal P_{\alpha,X}$ their port spaces, $\mathcal U_X$ a common latent
space, and $\widehat{\mathcal K}_X$ the physical future quotient.  A
common-source factorization consists of
\[
 C_X:\mathcal U_X\to\widehat{\mathcal K}_X,
 \qquad
 B_{\alpha,X}:\mathcal P_{\alpha,X}\to\mathcal U_X,
 \qquad
 \widetilde S_{\alpha,X}=C_XB_{\alpha,X}.
\]
Put
\[
 G_X=C_X^*C_X\succeq0,
 \qquad
 B_X(p_\alpha)_\alpha=\sum_\alpha B_{\alpha,X}p_\alpha.
\]
The factorization is \emph{non-tautological} when $C_X$, $G_X$, and the
loadings are independently generated by the physical common source rather
than defined by renaming the ranges of the target selectors.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Universal Grand Tensor]
\label{def:grand-tensor-master}
The cutoff Grand Tensor is the positive block operator
\[
 \boxed{
 \boldsymbol{\mathbb T}_{\Grand,X}^{\BR}
 :=B_X^*G_XB_X,}
\]
with sector blocks
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathbb T_{\alpha\beta,X}^{\BR}
 =B_{\alpha,X}^*G_XB_{\beta,X}.}
\]
The universal object is the compatible family over $X$, not one fixed finite
matrix.  Its local coefficient fibre may remain finite while its global
source, port, and quotient spaces grow.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact common-core transfer and its firewall]
\label{thm:grand-core-transfer}
Let $D_X\succeq0$ on $\mathcal U_X$ and
$E_{\alpha,X}\succeq0$ on $\mathcal P_{\alpha,X}$.  If
\[
 G_X\succeq g_XD_X,
 \qquad
 B_{\alpha,X}^*D_XB_{\alpha,X}
 \succeq b_{\alpha,X}E_{\alpha,X},
\]
then
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathbb T_{\alpha\alpha,X}^{\BR}
 \succeq g_Xb_{\alpha,X}E_{\alpha,X}.}
\]
Conversely,
\[
 \Ker B_{\alpha,X}\subseteq
 \Ker\mathbb T_{\alpha\alpha,X}^{\BR},
 \qquad
 0\preceq\mathbb T_{\alpha\alpha,X}^{\BR}
 \preceq\norm{G_X}B_{\alpha,X}^*B_{\alpha,X}.
\]
Thus a common-core estimate transfers only through a sector-specific loading
or observability estimate.  Cross-sector coherence cannot repair a vector
annihilated by its own loading.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Conjugate the core lower bound by $B_{\alpha,X}$.  The kernel and upper bound
follow from $0\preceq G_X\preceq\norm{G_X}I$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Physical source-cyclic subspace]
For one cutoff, suppress $X$ and let $G=G^*\succeq0$ and
$B:\mathcal P\to\mathcal U$.  Define
\[
 \mathcal U_{\rm cyc}
 :=\Span\{p(G)Bv:p\in\C[z],\ v\in\mathcal P\}.
\]
The triple $(\mathcal U,G,B)$ is source minimal when
$\mathcal U_{\rm cyc}=\mathcal U$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Physical-source completeness and minimal realization]
\label{thm:physical-source-completeness}
For every finite triple $(\mathcal U,G,B)$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item $\mathcal U_{\rm cyc}$ reduces $G$ and contains every state reachable
      from the declared physical ports by polynomial future response;
\item all source moments $B^*G^nB$ and all blocks $B^*f(G)B$ depend only on
      $G|_{\mathcal U_{\rm cyc}}$;
\item $\mathcal U_{\rm cyc}^\perp$ is invisible to the declared source
      correlations and may be removed when forming the minimal operational
      realization;
\item two finite source-minimal triples with identical all-order moments are
      related by a unique source-fixing unitary intertwining their future
      responses.
\end{enumerate}
Hence all declared moments determine the unique minimal common-source
realization up to unitary equivalence.  This is a reachable--observable
minimality theorem, not yet an assertion that an independently retained ambient
Hilbert space has no invisible direct summand.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Polynomial invariance makes $\mathcal U_{\rm cyc}$ invariant under the
self-adjoint $G$, hence reducing.  Compression proves the moment statements.
For uniqueness, define on polynomial source vectors
\[
 W_0\left(\sum_{j=0}^NG^jBv_j\right)
 :=\sum_{j=0}^NG'^jB'v_j.
\]
All pairwise inner products are finite linear combinations of the common
moments, so $W_0$ is well defined and isometric.  Source minimality makes its
domain and range dense; finite dimension makes the extension unitary.  The
constant and shifted polynomials give $WB=B'$ and $WG=G'W$.
\end{proof}

For the exact symmetry-resolved statement, let $0\preceq T=T^*\preceq I$ act on
a finite Hilbert space $\Hh$, let $S:\mathcal F\to\Hh$ be a finite source, and
let a finite group $K$ act unitarily by $U_g$ on $\Hh$ and by $\rho_g$ on the
port space $\mathcal F$.  Assume
\[
 [T,U_g]=0,\qquad U_gS=S\rho_g.
\]
For an irreducible representation $\pi$ of dimension $d_\pi$, put
\[
 P_\pi=\frac{d_\pi}{|K|}\sum_{g\in K}
       \overline{\chi_\pi(g)}U_g,
 \qquad
 M_k(g)=S^*U_gT^kS,
\]
\[
 M_k^{(\pi)}=S^*P_\pi T^kS
 =\frac{d_\pi}{|K|}\sum_g\overline{\chi_\pi(g)}M_k(g),
\]
and
\[
 \mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(r)}
 =[M_{i+j+r}^{(\pi)}]_{0\le i,j<n},\qquad r=0,1,2.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Character-Hankel primitive sector saturation]
\label{thm:primitive-sector-saturation}
Let
\[
 \mathcal K_{\pi,n}
 =\Span\{P_\pi T^j\Ran S:0\le j<n\},
 \qquad
 \mathcal K_{\pi,\infty}=\overline{\bigcup_n\mathcal K_{\pi,n}}.
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{
 \rank\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)}=\dim\mathcal K_{\pi,n}.}
\]
In particular, the represented multiplicity of $\pi$ at horizon $n$ is
$\rank\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)}/d_\pi$.  The sector survives in the minimal
physical realization exactly when some character-projected moment is nonzero.
If the ranks stabilize between $n$ and $n+1$, then
$\mathcal K_{\pi,n}=\mathcal K_{\pi,\infty}$ and the entire represented
$\pi$-future is reconstructed by finitely many transition blocks.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Define the controllability map
\[
 \mathcal C_{\pi,n}(f_0,\ldots,f_{n-1})
 =\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}P_\pi T^jSf_j.
\]
Commutation of $P_\pi$ and $T$ gives
$\mathcal C_{\pi,n}^*\mathcal C_{\pi,n}
=\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)}$.  The rank of a Gram matrix equals the dimension of
the span of its vectors, proving the first identity.  The source intertwining
law gives
\[
 U_hP_\pi T^jSf=P_\pi T^jS\rho_hf,
\]
so $\mathcal K_{\pi,n}$ is a $K$-invariant subspace of the $\pi$-isotypic
component.  It is therefore a direct sum of $d_\pi$-dimensional irreducible
carriers, which proves divisibility of the rank and the multiplicity formula.  If consecutive ranks agree, the nested spaces
$\mathcal K_{\pi,n}\subseteq\mathcal K_{\pi,n+1}$ are equal; then
$T\mathcal K_{\pi,n}\subseteq\mathcal K_{\pi,n+1}=\mathcal K_{\pi,n}$, so all
later Krylov layers coincide.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact character-sector leakage]
\label{thm:character-leakage-master}
On $\mathcal E_{\pi,n}=\Ran\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)}$, define
\[
 A_{\pi,n}
 =(\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)})^{\dagger/2}
   \mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(1)}
  (\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)})^{\dagger/2}
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathbb V_{\pi,n}
 =(\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)})^{\dagger/2}
   \mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(2)}
  (\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)})^{\dagger/2}
 -A_{\pi,n}^2.}
\]
Then $\mathbb V_{\pi,n}\succeq0$ and
\[
 \boxed{
 \rank\mathbb V_{\pi,n}
 =\dim(\mathcal K_{\pi,n+1}\ominus\mathcal K_{\pi,n}).}
\]
It vanishes exactly when the finite head is invariant, equivalently when the
represented $\pi$ sector is saturated.  Every nonzero singular vector of
$\mathbb V_{\pi,n}$ constructs an explicit next missing source direction.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Whiten $\mathcal C_{\pi,n}$ by
$(\mathbb H_{\pi,n}^{(0)})^{\dagger/2}$ to obtain an isometry $V_{\pi,n}$ onto
$\mathcal K_{\pi,n}$.  Then $A_{\pi,n}=V_{\pi,n}^*TV_{\pi,n}$.  If $P_n$ is
the projection onto this Krylov head and $Q_n$ its orthogonal complement inside
$\mathcal K_{\pi,\infty}$, direct expansion gives
\[
 \mathbb V_{\pi,n}
 =V_{\pi,n}^*TQ_nTV_{\pi,n}=R_{\pi,n}^*R_{\pi,n},
 \qquad R_{\pi,n}=Q_nTV_{\pi,n}.
\]
Its range is exactly
$\mathcal K_{\pi,n+1}\ominus\mathcal K_{\pi,n}$, proving positivity, rank, and
the closure equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[Ambient sector saturation is not data-identifiable]
\label{cth:ambient-source-master}
Let $(\Hh,U,T,S)$ be any equivariant source realization and let
$\mathcal Z\ne0$ carry an arbitrary unitary representation $V$ and positive
contraction $A$ commuting with it.  Put
\[
 \Hh'=\Hh\oplus\mathcal Z,
 \quad U'_g=U_g\oplus V_g,
 \quad T'=T\oplus A,
 \quad S'=S\oplus0.
\]
Then every declared transition block is unchanged:
\[
 (S')^*U'_g(T')^kS'=S^*U_gT^kS
 \qquad(g\in K,\ k\ge0),
\]
while $\mathcal Z$ may contain arbitrary additional scalar, character,
topological, or soft future sectors.  No theorem from the declared moments
alone can identify the ambient retained space with the minimal source-cyclic
space.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
For every source vector, $(T')^kS'f=T^kSf\oplus0$; applying $U'_g$ and then
$(S')^*$ gives the identity.  The representation and spectrum on
$\mathcal Z$ are otherwise unconstrained.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform finite-horizon saturation is regulator stable]
\label{thm:source-regulator-master}
Let $T_\epsilon\to T$ and $S_\epsilon\to S$ in norm on one finite comparison
space.  Define
\[
 \mathcal C_N(T_\epsilon,S_\epsilon)(f_0,\ldots,f_{N-1})
 :=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}T_\epsilon^jS_\epsilon f_j.
\]
Suppose that for some fixed horizon $N$ and $c>0$,
\[
 \mathcal C_N(T_\epsilon,S_\epsilon)
 \mathcal C_N(T_\epsilon,S_\epsilon)^*\succeq cI
\]
for every $\epsilon$.  Then the same inequality holds in the limit and $S$ is
cyclic for $T$ at horizon $N$.  The conclusion holds isotypically when the
character projectors also converge.  Without a uniform lower bound, cyclicity
may collapse even in dimension two.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The finite-horizon controllability operators converge in norm, hence so do
their frame operators.  The cone $\{X:X\succeq cI\}$ is norm closed, so the
limit frame operator is bounded below and therefore surjective in finite
dimension.  For the failure statement take
$T=\operatorname{diag}(0,1/2)$ and
$s_\epsilon=(e_1+\epsilon e_2)/\sqrt{1+\epsilon^2}$.  For every
$\epsilon>0$, $s_\epsilon$ and $Ts_\epsilon$ span $\C^2$, but at
$\epsilon=0$ the cyclic space is only $\C e_1$; the least frame eigenvalue
tends to zero.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Corrected common-action naturality}

Let $\mathfrak A_{\rm obs}$ be a finite-dimensional space of observable action
germs and let $a_0$ span a normalized common line, so the normalized effective
coefficient functionals satisfy $\ell_j(a_0)=1$.

\begin{theorem}[Common-action naturality, Legendre reciprocity, and measurable splitting]
\label{thm:naturality-splitting}
The following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item If the actual action class is $[a]=\chi[a_0]$, then every normalized
      linear descendant has coefficient $\chi$.  More generally, a fixed
      homogeneous operation of degree $p_j$ produces coefficient $\chi^{p_j}$.
\item For a nondegenerate symmetric quadratic form $B$ and
      \[
        L_\chi(q,v)=\frac\chi2B(v,v)-\chi V(q),
      \]
      the Legendre transform is
      \[
        H_\chi(q,p)=\frac1{2\chi}B^{-1}(p,p)+\chi V(q).
      \]
      Thus one common action coefficient gives reciprocal Hamiltonian
      coefficients $a=\chi^{-1}$ and $b=\chi$, hence $ab=1$.
\item Let two retained descendants have self-adjoint loading difference
      $D=B_1-B_2$ on their minimal action-cyclic space.  Exactly one of the
      following occurs:
      \[
      D=0;\qquad D=\delta I\ne0;\qquad D\notin\R I.
      \]
      The middle case is a second scalar coupling and need not possess a branch
      discriminator.  In the last case there is a finite source polynomial
      $y_p=p(T)S$ with
      \[
        \boxed{
        \Delta_D(p)
        =\norm{y_p}^2\norm{Dy_p}^2
         -|\langle y_p,Dy_p\rangle|^2>0,}
      \]
      where $P_{y_p}$ is the orthogonal projection onto $\C y_p$, and
      $(I-P_{y_p})Dy_p$ is an explicit retained branch record.  The defect
      is a finite combination of inserted blocks
      $S^*T^iDT^jS$, $S^*T^iD^2T^jS$, and $S^*T^{i+j}S$.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently the informal ``unequal coefficients imply a discriminator''
statement is valid only after a second scalar coupling and unretained affine
counterterms have been excluded or represented as explicit action directions.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Linearity and homogeneity prove the first claim.  For the second, the momentum
is $p=\chi Bv$, so $v=\chi^{-1}B^{-1}p$; substitution in
$p(v)-L_\chi$ gives the displayed Hamiltonian.  For the third, the scalar
operators form the one-dimensional subspace $\R I$, giving the exhaustive
trichotomy.  If $D$ is non-scalar and the Cauchy--Schwarz defect vanished for
every polynomial source vector, then every vector in the dense polynomial
source orbit would be an eigenvector.  Applying this to two independent
vectors and their sum forces one common eigenvalue; boundedness would give
$D=\delta I$, a contradiction.  Expanding the defect gives the stated inserted
transition blocks.
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[A second scalar coupling needs no branch record]
\label{cth:scalar-coupling-no-record-master}
Let the retained action environment be one dimensional and let
\[
 \mathscr A_{\alpha,\beta}=\alpha K+\beta V,
 \qquad \alpha\ne\beta,
\]
for two fixed nonzero effective functionals $K,V$.  Then every retained
self-adjoint effect is a scalar multiple of the identity, so no nontrivial
branch discriminator exists, while the two effective coefficients remain
independent.  Therefore ``unequal coefficients imply a retained branch
record'' is false unless a second scalar action direction and affine
counterterms have first been excluded or explicitly physicalized.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Every operator on a one-dimensional Hilbert space is scalar, whereas
$\alpha$ and $\beta$ are independent coordinates in the two-dimensional
action space $\Span\{K,V\}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why a fixed finite matrix cannot be universal]
If the joint port dimension grows with prime support, lattice volume, fluid
resolution, jet order, Betti number, or input length, no fixed finite global
carrier can be coercive on every cutoff.  Universality means one compatible
formula, one local coefficient architecture, and explicitly growing minimal
source realizations.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================================
\section{Exact arithmetic blocks of the Grand Tensor}
\label{sec:arithmetic-blocks}
% =============================================================================

\begin{definition}[Reflected zero heat]
Let $\chi$ be primitive and let $\Xi_\chi$ be its standard pole-cleared
completed Dirichlet function.  For its nontrivial zeros $\rho$, counted with
multiplicity, define
\[
 \boxed{
 u_\chi(t,\tau)
 =\frac1{\sqrt{4\pi t}}
 \sum_{\Xi_\chi(\rho)=0}m_\rho
 \exp\!\left[-\frac{(\tau-\Im\rho
 +i(\Re\rho-\tfrac12))^2}{4t}\right].}
\]
The reflection symmetry of the zero set makes $u_\chi$ real valued.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Gaussian heat criterion for Dirichlet GRH]
\label{thm:GRH-heat-master}
For a primitive Dirichlet character $\chi$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item every nontrivial zero of $\Xi_\chi$ has real part $1/2$;
\item $u_\chi(t,\tau)\ge0$ for every $t>0$ and $\tau\in\R$;
\item the reflected $2\times2$ affine moment matrix generated by the ports
      $1$ and $s-1/2$ is positive semidefinite at every Gaussian scale and
      center;
\item
      \[
       u_\chi\,\partial_\tau^2u_\chi
       -(\partial_\tau u_\chi)^2
       +\frac1{2t}u_\chi^2\ge0
      \]
      together with $u_\chi\ge0$ for every $t,\tau$.
\end{enumerate}
For the principal zeta channel, the pole contribution must be cleared exactly
before applying the criterion.  RH is the corresponding principal-channel
specialization; GRH is the conjunction over the chosen primitive character
family.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof from Gaussian orbit exposure]
If all zeros lie on the line, every summand is the ordinary positive heat
kernel centered at $\Im\rho$, proving positivity.  Conversely, an off-line
reflection pair $1/2\pm c+i\gamma$ contributes
\[
 \frac{2}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}
 e^{(c^2-x^2)/(4t)}\cos\!\left(\frac{cx}{2t}\right),
 \qquad x=\tau-\gamma.
\]
Choosing $x=2\pi t/c$ makes this contribution negative with exponential
magnitude $e^{c^2/(4t)+O(t)}$.  A Gaussian outer-exposure argument chooses an
off-line orbit of maximal horizontal displacement and a sequence of centers
for which all competing orbits are exponentially smaller.  Positivity then
fails.  This proves \textup{(i)}$\Leftrightarrow$\textup{(ii)}.
The affine matrix is the Gram of $1$ and $s-1/2$ under the reflected zero
functional.  Direct differentiation of the Gaussian gives its determinant,
up to a positive scalar, as the displayed heat-semiconvexity expression,
proving the remaining equivalences.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact prime-side heat identity]
\label{thm:prime-side-heat-master}
There is a completely explicit conductor--gamma--cleared-pole background
$\mathfrak G_\chi(t,\tau)$ such that
\[
 \boxed{
 u_\chi(t,\tau)
 =\mathfrak G_\chi(t,\tau)
 -\frac1\pi\Re\sum_{n\ge1}
 \frac{\chi(n)\Lambda(n)}{n^{1/2+i\tau}}
 e^{-t(\log n)^2}.}
\]
Thus GRH is transferred exactly to a phase-sensitive prime-side inequality,
not to an ordinary lower frame bound.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Insert the reflection-symmetric Gaussian test into the symmetric explicit
formula for the completed Dirichlet function.  Its inverse Mellin transform is
the log-Gaussian
$x^{-1/2-i\tau}(4\pi t)^{-1/2}e^{-(\log x)^2/(4t)}$.  The archimedean,
conductor, and cleared-pole terms form $\mathfrak G_\chi$; the prime powers
give the displayed von Mangoldt wave.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Heat-affine prime port]
For $t>0$, $\tau\in\R$, and $\theta\in\R/\Z$, put
\[
 \mathcal P_\chi(t,\tau,\theta)
 :=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\chi(n)\Lambda(n)}{n^{1/2+i\tau}}
 e^{-t(\log n)^2}e^{2\pi in\theta}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact arithmetic Grand-Tensor readouts]
\label{thm:arithmetic-grand-readouts}
The anchored bi-reflected port loading of $\mathcal P_\chi$ is a sector of
\Cref{def:grand-tensor-master}.  Its anchor cross-entry is the order-one prime
wave in \Cref{thm:prime-side-heat-master}.  Its normal and anomalous quadratic
blocks are, respectively,
\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^1\mathcal P_\chi(t,\tau_1,\theta)
 \overline{\mathcal P_\psi(t,\tau_2,\theta)}e^{-2\pi ih\theta}\dd\theta
 =\sum_{m-n=h}a_m\overline{b_n},}
\]
\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^1\mathcal P_\chi(t,\tau_1,\theta)
 \mathcal P_\psi(t,\tau_2,\theta)e^{-2\pi iN\theta}\dd\theta
 =\sum_{m+n=N}a_mb_n,}
\]
where $a_m$ and $b_n$ are the displayed heat-Mellin von Mangoldt
coefficients.  Hence fixed differences and fixed sums are different tensor
grades of one common port family.  The order-one GRH inequality has no purely
algebraic implication for either order-two lower bound.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Absolute convergence permits termwise integration.  Fourier orthogonality
imposes $m-n=h$ in the normal product and $m+n=N$ in the anomalous product.
The last sentence follows from finite Fourier polynomials with equal value at
$\theta=0$ but different quadratic coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Twin and Goldbach heat selectors]
For the trivial character and zero Mellin center, define
\[
 \mathcal D_2(t)
 =\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\Lambda(n)\Lambda(n+2)}{\sqrt{n(n+2)}}
 e^{-t[(\log n)^2+(\log(n+2))^2]},
\]
\[
 \mathcal H_N(t)
 =\sum_{m+n=N}\frac{\Lambda(m)\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt{mn}}
 e^{-t[(\log m)^2+(\log n)^2]}.
\]
Both are nonnegative exact Grand-Tensor readouts.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Twin-prime heat sufficiency]
\label{thm:twin-heat-master}
The total contribution to $\mathcal D_2(t)$ from terms for which at least one
of $n,n+2$ is a proper prime power is uniformly bounded for $0<t\le1$.
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal D_2(t)\longrightarrow\infty\quad(t\downarrow0)
 \ \Longrightarrow\ 
 \text{infinitely many twin-prime pairs}.}
\]
The divergence certificate is not proved by the universal Grand Tensor.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $n=p^k$, $k\ge2$, use $\sqrt{n(n+2)}\ge n$ and
$\Lambda(n+2)\le\log(n+2)$.  The resulting majorant is a constant multiple
of
$\sum_p\sum_{k\ge2}k(\log p)^2p^{-k}<\infty$; the case where $n+2$ is a
proper prime power is identical.  If only finitely many genuine twin pairs
existed, their heat contribution would also remain bounded.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Binary-Goldbach heat sufficiency]
\label{thm:Goldbach-heat-master}
Uniformly in $t>0$, the contribution to $\mathcal H_N(t)$ from pairs with at
least one proper prime-power summand is
\[
 O\!\left(N^{-1/4}(\log N)^2\right).
\]
If there are $c>0$ and choices $t_N>0$ such that
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal H_N(t_N)\ge c}
\]
for every sufficiently large even $N$, then every sufficiently large even
integer is a sum of two primes.  The uniform lower bound is an open
sector-loading certificate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
There are $O(\sqrt N)$ proper prime powers at most $N$.  For any set
$S\subset\{1,\ldots,N-1\}$ of cardinality $K$,
\[
 \sum_{m\in S}\frac1{\sqrt{m(N-m)}}
 \ll\frac{\sqrt K}{\sqrt N}.
\]
Take $K=O(\sqrt N)$ and bound both von Mangoldt factors by $\log N$; the heat
factors only reduce the sum.  A fixed positive lower bound eventually exceeds
this contamination, forcing a term with both summands prime.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Arithmetic separation theorem]
The Grand Tensor unifies the ports, reflections, nuisance shorting, and
source-completeness language.  It does not identify the decisive estimates:
RH/GRH require reflected prime-side cancellation, twin primes require
unbounded fixed-difference prime support, and Goldbach requires a uniform
moving-sum lower bound.  These are distinct loadings and remain distinct even
inside one common-source family.
\end{assessment}

% =============================================================================
\section{Millennium-problem and major-sector interfaces}
\label{sec:millennium-interface}
% =============================================================================

\begin{assessment}[Current external status]
The Clay list has seven Millennium Prize Problems.  The Poincar\'e conjecture
is the solved benchmark; RH, Yang--Mills existence and mass gap,
Navier--Stokes existence and smoothness, Birch--Swinnerton--Dyer, Hodge, and
$P$ versus $NP$ remain target sectors \cite{ClayMillennium}.  The statements below are interfaces
and conditional closure theorems.  They are deliberately not merged into the
spacetime flagship.
\end{assessment}

\subsection{Yang--Mills: physical loop-complexity regeneration}

Let $T=e^{-\tau H}$ be a positive centered gauge-invariant transfer
contraction, split relative to a finite source-Hankel head as
\[
 T=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\B^*&D\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad a=\norm A,\quad b=\norm B.
\]
A physical loop-complexity filtration is a decomposition fixed before any
spectral or Lanczos analysis,
\[
 \mathcal H_{\rm neu}=\bigoplus_{j\ge0}\mathcal E_j,
 \qquad \Pi_j:\mathcal H_{\rm neu}\to\mathcal E_j,
\]
whose labels are retained loop-size, representation, multi-loop, center, or
cohomology records.  It is nearest-neighbour when
\[
 \Pi_iT\Pi_j=0\qquad(|i-j|>1).
\]
For a finite source $S:\C^m\to\mathcal E_0$ put
\[
 \mathcal K_n(S)=\Span\{T^k\Ran S:0\le k<n\},
 \qquad
 \mathcal G_n=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathcal E_j,
\]
and let $P_n$ be the projection onto $\mathcal K_n(S)$.

\begin{theorem}[Physical-shell localization of the Hankel source]
\label{thm:YM-physical-shell-master}
If the physical filtration is nearest-neighbour, then for every $n\ge1$,
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal K_n(S)\subseteq\mathcal G_n,}
\]
and for the exact Hankel leakage $R_n=(I-P_n)TP_n$,
\[
 \boxed{\Ran R_n\subseteq
 \mathcal G_{n+1}\ominus\mathcal K_n(S).}
\]
If the predeclared physical shell atlas is source cyclic through level $n$,
so that $\mathcal K_n(S)=\mathcal G_n$, then
\[
 \boxed{\Ran R_n\subseteq\mathcal E_n.}
\]
If, in addition, every resolved one-slab history enlarges geometric support by
at most a fixed collar $R_0$, the first residual is supported in the
$R_0$-collar of the initial source and the $n$th physical shell in its
$nR_0$-collar.  Thus the first missing source is a predeclared local physical
history, not merely an abstract Ritz vector.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since $\Ran S\subseteq\mathcal E_0$, the first inclusion holds for $n=1$.
If $T^k\Ran S\subseteq\mathcal G_{k+1}$, nearest-neighbour propagation gives
$T^{k+1}\Ran S\subseteq T\mathcal G_{k+1}\subseteq\mathcal G_{k+2}$.
Induction proves $\mathcal K_n(S)\subseteq\mathcal G_n$.  Hence
$TP_n\mathcal H\subseteq\mathcal G_{n+1}$; subtracting its component in
$\mathcal K_n(S)$ proves the leakage inclusion.  Under
$\mathcal K_n(S)=\mathcal G_n$, the orthogonal difference
$\mathcal G_{n+1}\ominus\mathcal G_n$ is $\mathcal E_n$.  The collar statement
follows by the same induction applied to geometric supports.
\end{proof}

Let a resolved complexity process have growth, reduction, stay, and direct
regeneration probabilities
\[
 p,q,r,\kappa\ge0,
 \qquad p+q+r+\kappa=1.
\]
At a finite tail cutoff $N$, let $K_N^{p,q,r}$ be the symmetric tridiagonal
matrix with
\[
 (K_N)_{jj}=r\ (j<N),
 \qquad (K_N)_{NN}=r+p,
 \qquad (K_N)_{j,j+1}=\sqrt{pq}.
\]
A positive operator-valued tail $D_N$ is a KMS fibre extension of this process
when
\[
 \norm{\Pi_iD_N\Pi_j}\le (K_N^{p,q,r})_{ij}.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Uniform KMS birth--death tail bound]
\label{thm:YM-birth-death-tail-master}
Every such KMS fibre extension satisfies
\[
 \boxed{\norm{D_N}\le1-\kappa.}
\]
If $q\ge p$, then the stronger uniform estimate holds:
\[
 \boxed{
 \norm{D_N}\le r+2\sqrt{pq}
 =1-\kappa-(\sqrt q-\sqrt p)^2.}
\]
Both estimates are independent of the maximal complexity $N$ and of all
internal shell multiplicities.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $x=\sum_jx_j$ with $x_j\in\mathcal E_j$, put $y_j=\norm{x_j}$.  Positivity
and the block bounds give
\[
 \langle x,D_Nx\rangle
 \le\sum_{i,j}(K_N)_{ij}y_iy_j
 \le\norm{K_N}\norm{x}^2,
\]
so $\norm{D_N}\le\norm{K_N}$.  For $p,q>0$, let
$t=\sqrt{p/q}$ and $w_j=t^j$.  Every interior and reflected top row satisfies
$(K_Nw)_j/w_j=1-\kappa$, while the bottom quotient is at most this value.
Collatz--Wielandt therefore gives $\rho(K_N)\le1-\kappa$.  Since $K_N$ is real
symmetric and entrywise nonnegative, $\norm{K_N}=\rho(K_N)$.  The degenerate
cases follow by continuity.  If $q\ge p$, every row sum is at most
$r+2\sqrt{pq}$, including the reflected top row because
$p+\sqrt{pq}\le2\sqrt{pq}$.  The row-sum bound gives the second estimate, and
$1-r-2\sqrt{pq}=\kappa+(\sqrt q-\sqrt p)^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[Conservative loop diffusion cannot produce a uniform mass]
\label{cth:YM-loop-diffusion-master}
Assume $\kappa=0$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item If $p=q>0$, then
      \[
       \lim_{N\to\infty}\norm{K_N^{p,p,1-2p}}=1.
      \]
\item If $p>q\ge0$, then
      \[
       \lim_{N\to\infty}\norm{K_N^{p,q,1-p-q}}=1.
      \]
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, in the absence of direct regeneration, a volume-uniform
birth--death tail gap requires strict return bias $q>p$.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
For $p=q$, take a unit vector approximately constant on a growing interval of
shells away from the lower boundary.  All interior rows have sum one and only
the two ends of the interval contribute a defect, so its Rayleigh quotient
tends to one.  For $p>q>0$, take $w_j=(p/q)^{j/2}$.  One has $K_Nw=w$ on every
row except the bottom row, while $\norm w$ is dominated by the top shells;
therefore $\norm{(K_N-I)w}/\norm w\to0$.  If $q=0$, a vector supported at the
reflected top shell gives the same conclusion.  Since every $K_N$ is a
contraction, the norms tend to one.
\end{proof}

Define the physical regeneration margin
\[
 \mu_{\rm reg}=
 \begin{cases}
 \kappa+(\sqrt q-\sqrt p)^2,&q\ge p,\\
 \kappa,&q<p.
 \end{cases}
\]
Let $D^{\rm bd}$ be the KMS fibre extension generated by the predeclared
complexity record and let
\[
 \delta_{\rm comp}=\norm{D-D^{\rm bd}},
 \qquad
 \mu_{\rm eff}=\mu_{\rm reg}-\delta_{\rm comp}.
\]
Then \Cref{thm:YM-birth-death-tail-master} and the triangle inequality give
\[
 \norm D\le1-\mu_{\rm eff}.
\]
The defect $\delta_{\rm comp}$ measures unresolved physical histories rather
than a fit to the low eigenvalue; a large defect requires enlargement of the
source atlas.

\begin{theorem}[Explicit regulated neutral mass criterion]
\label{thm:YM-regulated-master}
If
\[
 a<1,\qquad \mu_{\rm eff}>0,
 \qquad b^2<(1-a)\mu_{\rm eff},
\]
then
\[
 \boxed{
 \norm T\le q_{\rm reg}
 :=\frac{a+1-\mu_{\rm eff}
 +\sqrt{(a-1+\mu_{\rm eff})^2+4b^2}}2<1,}
\]
and
\[
 \boxed{\Delta_{\rm reg}\ge-\tau^{-1}\log q_{\rm reg}>0.}
\]
If the invariant-loop source family is future cyclic and the constants are
uniform in volume and lattice spacing, the bound holds on the complete
centered finite-lattice gauge-invariant Hilbert space.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The uniform tail theorem and the source-defect estimate above give
$\norm D\le1-\mu_{\rm eff}$.  The norm of a positive $2\times2$ block
operator is bounded by the larger eigenvalue of the scalar matrix
$\begin{psmallmatrix}a&b\\b&1-\mu_{\rm eff}\end{psmallmatrix}$, which is
$q_{\rm reg}$.  The strict determinant condition is exactly
$b^2<(1-a)\mu_{\rm eff}$ and places the larger root below one.  The transfer
relation $T=e^{-\tau H}$ gives the mass bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[Why this is not yet the Clay theorem]
A four-dimensional Yang--Mills solution still requires the actual uniform
$p,q,\kappa,\delta_{\rm comp}$, an independent center-surface pressure
estimate, a nontrivial Osterwalder--Schrader continuum limit, a local
invariant net, a unique vacuum, and passage of the same positive physical
mass to that limit.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Navier--Stokes: the exact relative-form certificate}

Let $A=-P\Delta$ be the Stokes operator on mean-zero divergence-free fields
on $\Torus^3$, $B(u,v)=P((u\cdot\nabla)v)$, and for $s>5/2$ put
\[
 E_s=\frac12\norm{A^{s/2}u}_2^2,
 \qquad D_s=\norm{A^{(s+1)/2}u}_2^2.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Navier--Stokes continuation]
\label{thm:NS-relative-master}
Suppose on every finite interval of existence there are $\theta\in(0,1]$ and
$k\in L^1_{\rm loc}$ such that
\[
 \left|\langle A^{s/2}B(u,u),A^{s/2}u\rangle\right|
 \le(1-\theta)\nu D_s+k(t)E_s.
\]
Then, with $K(t)=\int_0^tk(r)\dd r$,
\[
 \boxed{
 e^{-K(t)}E_s(t)
 +\theta\nu\int_0^te^{-K(r)}D_s(r)\dd r
 \le E_s(0).}
\]
A global estimate of this form implies global smoothness for smooth data.
Generic positive Schur shorting cannot manufacture this estimate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The $H^s$ energy identity is
$E_s'+\nu D_s=-\Re\langle A^{s/2}B(u,u),A^{s/2}u\rangle$.
Insert the hypothesis and apply the integrating factor $e^{-K(t)}$.
Standard local continuation for $s>5/2$ excludes finite-time breakdown while
$E_s$ remains finite.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Birch--Swinnerton--Dyer: analytic jets and the missing comparison}

Let $\mathscr R_m=\C[z]/(z^m)$ and let $M_m(F)$ be multiplication by an
analytic germ $F$ modulo $z^m$.  Put
$\mathbb J_m(F)=M_m(F)^*M_m(F)$.

\begin{theorem}[Exact analytic-rank selector]
\label{thm:BSD-jet-master}
If $r=\operatorname{ord}_{z=0}F$, then
\[
 \boxed{\dim\Ker\mathbb J_m(F)=\min\{r,m\}.}
\]
For an elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$ and
$F_E(z)=\Lambda(E,1+z)$, this nullity equals
$\min\{r_{\rm an}(E),m\}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $F=z^rg$ with $g(0)\ne0$.  Multiplication by $g$ is invertible in the
local algebra, so the kernel is that of multiplication by $z^r$, namely the
span of $z^{m-r},\ldots,z^{m-1}$ when $r<m$ and the whole space when
$r\ge m$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[BSD common-source comparison bridge]
\label{thm:BSD-bridge-master}
Fix an elliptic curve $E$.  Suppose an independently constructed
Mordell--Weil/Selmer loading produces a finite source-minimal operator triple
whose distinguished zero space has dimension $r_{\rm alg}(E)$, and suppose
its all-order common-source moments are unitarily identified with the
source-minimal analytic jet realization of \Cref{thm:BSD-jet-master} for
some $m>\max(r_{\rm an},r_{\rm alg})$.  Then
$r_{\rm an}(E)=r_{\rm alg}(E)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Physical-source completeness gives a source-fixing unitary equivalence of the
two minimal realizations and therefore equality of their distinguished zero
multiplicities.  The jet nullity is $r_{\rm an}$ and the independent
arithmetic zero space has dimension $r_{\rm alg}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[BSD scope]
The moment identification in \Cref{thm:BSD-bridge-master} is the missing
arithmetic theorem, not a formal consequence of putting two kernels in one
direct sum.  The leading-coefficient, regulator, and Tate--Shafarevich parts
of BSD require further arithmetic data beyond rank equality.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Hodge: harmonic shorting and cycle observability}

\begin{theorem}[Hodge cycle-observability equivalence]
\label{thm:Hodge-master}
Let $X$ be a smooth complex projective variety and fix codimension $p$.
For rational algebraic cycles $Z_1,\ldots,Z_m$, let
\[
 \mathcal Z_X:\C^m\to H_{\rm Hdg}^{2p}(X;\C),
 \qquad c\mapsto\sum_jc_j[Z_j].
\]
The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item the classes $[Z_j]$ span the Hodge sector;
\item $\mathcal Z_X$ is surjective;
\item $\mathcal Z_X\mathcal Z_X^*$ is positive definite on the Hodge sector;
\item $\mathcal Z_X\mathcal Z_X^*\succeq c_XI$ for some $c_X>0$.
\end{enumerate}
The Hodge conjecture for $(X,p)$ is equivalent to the existence of a finite
family of rational cycles satisfying these conditions.  Shorting exact and
coexact forms gives harmonic projection but does not by itself prove cycle
surjectivity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
In finite dimensions, spanning is surjectivity, which is equivalent to
injectivity of $\mathcal Z_X^*$.  Since
$\langle h,\mathcal Z_X\mathcal Z_X^*h\rangle=
\norm{\mathcal Z_X^*h}^2$, this is positive definiteness, equivalent to a
positive least eigenvalue.  A finite rational spanning family is exactly the
fixed-sector Hodge assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$P$ versus $NP$}{P versus NP}: resource-faithful loading, not storage dimension}

\begin{proposition}[Ambient record dimension firewall]
\label{prop:PvsNP-dimension-master}
Representing all $2^n$ Boolean inputs by orthogonal vectors forces ambient
dimension at least $2^n$ but implies no circuit lower bound.  The computational
basis has this dimension while the identity and parity functions have linear
size circuits.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional resource-faithful complexity bridge]
\label{thm:PvsNP-bridge-master}
Let $L$ be an explicit NP-complete language and $f_n$ its length-$n$ Boolean
family.  Suppose an independently defined Grand-Tensor loading has a resource
functional $R_n(f)$ and a proved comparison
\[
 \operatorname{CircuitSize}(f)\ge R_n(f)/p(n)
\]
for one polynomial $p$, and suppose the physical-source analysis proves
$R_n(f_n)$ grows faster than every polynomial.  Then
$L\notin P/{\rm poly}$, hence $P\ne NP$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The comparison gives superpolynomial circuit size for the explicit NP-complete
family.  A language in $P$ has polynomial-size circuit families, so $L\notin
P$ and $P\ne NP$; in fact the conclusion is the stronger nonuniform
separation stated.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[Complexity scope]
The missing theorem is the resource comparison itself plus a superpolynomial
loading bound for an explicit NP-complete family.  Phase retention, Hilbert
space dimension, or a generic Schur complement is not a standard resource
measure.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Poincar\'e: solved external benchmark for scale flow and surgery}

\begin{proposition}[Perelman benchmark interface]
\label{prop:Poincare-benchmark}
Perelman's Ricci-flow solution of the Poincar\'e conjecture
\cite{PerelmanEntropy,PerelmanSurgery} is used only as an
external benchmark for a valid scale-flow module: a geometrically defined
monotone entropy, canonical-neighbourhood control, no collapsing, and
controlled surgery with a summable entropy/coercivity budget.  Any renewal
sector admitting analogous objects can be represented by a scale-indexed
Grand Tensor, but this representation neither reproves Poincar\'e nor exports
Perelman's geometric estimates to arithmetic, gauge, fluid, or complexity
sectors.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Encoding a monotone functional and its surgery jumps as a scalar or operator
port is formal.  The monotonicity, canonical-neighbourhood theorem, no
collapsing, and surgery estimates are the problem-specific geometric input.
Their existence in Ricci flow proves compatibility of the architecture, not
transfer to a different evolution.
\end{proof}

\section{Finite gauge--matter companion module}
\label{sec:gauge-matter-companion}
% =============================================================================

\begin{assessment}[Nearest broader finite theorem]
The gauge--matter branch is a companion to, not a premise of, spacetime
emergence.  The cumulative finite source calculations provide a Pati--Salam
parent, descent to one Standard-Model generation, the hypercharge direction
$Y=T_R^3+(B-L)/2$, canonical level $k_Y=5/3$, anomaly cancellation, the
global $\bigl(SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1)_Y\bigr)/\Z_6$ quotient, a
one-Higgs-doublet representation, and four multiplicity-one Yukawa channel
types.  Under one common current writer and KMS balance they give the bare
matching relations $g_1=g_2=g_3$ and $\sin^2\theta_W=3/8$.
\end{assessment}

\begin{theorem}[Finite gauge--matter source certificate bridge]
\label{thm:finite-SM-bridge}
Suppose one microscopic retained Store--Read instrument satisfies the finite
current-writer, KMS-balance, incidence-Ward, rank-one Majorana, Higgs-reality,
and source-response nondegeneracy certificates isolated in the gauge--matter
branch.  Then its minimal source-cyclic gauge--matter realization contains the
one-generation representation and finite coupling relations listed above,
with every unused representation sector removed by
\Cref{thm:primitive-sector-saturation}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each finite certificate fixes one independently typed loading into the common
source: the current writer fixes the gauge Gram, KMS balance identifies the
left/right normalization, incidence descent imposes the Ward-compatible
subgroup, the atomic Majorana source fixes the chiral stabilizer and
hypercharge direction, and Higgs reality reduces the bi-doublet to one
complex doublet.  Source completeness removes all future-invisible copies;
sector saturation retains precisely the loaded irreducible sectors.  The
finite representation and trace computations then give the stated quotient,
anomalies, and matching values.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[Continuum Standard Model]
The finite theorem does not construct an interacting renormalized continuum
Standard Model, determine running low-energy couplings and masses, prove a
Higgs potential minimum, or derive observed flavour parameters.  Those are
additional dynamical and scaling problems.
\end{firewall}

\begin{proposition}[Master no-overstatement boundary]
\label{prop:master-no-overstatement}
The strengthened master theorem does not assert:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item that every recurrent process has the analytic Hamiltonian cross-support,
      reversible score control, or quantitative resource bounds required by the
      Store--control entrance;
\item that predictive refinement completeness implies reversible control;
\item that a zero Store-frequency rebit block is erased or complexified;
\item that a one-step score effect determines repeated score dynamics, or that
      matching clock and geometry Hessians or marginal moments---without an
      admitted amplitude-level bridge---proves one physical action source;
\item that a rank-one complete reset cut proves metricity or removes torsion;
\item that the determinant-dual coefficient $1/2$ alone proves $ab=1$;
\item that ESSC or heat-bath locality supplies the gravitational normal
      constraint;
\item that finite HDA-shaped coefficients imply a continuum Einstein equation
      without compactness, concentration, and first-variation control;
\item that universal relative matter coupling fixes a gravity--matter action
      orbit;
\item that the orientation line selects one orientation or a thermodynamic
      arrow;
\item that $G$ or $\Lambda$ is fixed without TT and absolute-loading
      metrology;
\item that source saturation for a declared writer/read family excludes an
      ambient future-invisible direct summand;
\item that any unsolved Millennium problem or prime conjecture is proved here.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Items \textup{(1)--(3)} are the control and zero-mode firewalls of
\Cref{ass:predictive-clock-entry,thm:analytic-store-polar-master}.  Item
\textup{(4)} is
\Cref{prop:score-effect-depth-no-go-master,cth:equal-Hessian-not-source-master,fire:cross-source-nonidentifiability-master}.  Item
\textup{(5)} is \Cref{thm:physical-connection-master}.  Item \textup{(6)} is
\Cref{thm:Clifford-common-action-master}.  Items \textup{(7)--(8)} are
\Cref{thm:ESSC-independence-master,thm:Einstein-extension-master}.  Item
\textup{(9)} is \Cref{thm:moving-frame-matter-master}; typed-sector contrast is
compatible with the same total HDA.  Item \textup{(10)} is
\Cref{thm:orientation-phase-master}.  Item \textup{(11)} is
\Cref{thm:metrology-master}.  Item \textup{(12)} is
\Cref{cth:ambient-source-master}.  Item \textup{(13)} follows from the open
sector certificates in \Cref{sec:millennium-interface}.
\end{proof}

\clearpage

% =============================================================================
\section{Certificate-level Lorentzian route retained for a self-contained proof audit}
% =============================================================================

\begin{assessment}[Role of the retained certificate route]
The following sections preserve the complete earlier certificate chain.  They
remain a rigorous sufficient audit of predictive reconstruction, the signed
cover, the pressure clock, the projective Clifford factor, the minimal external
$(3+1)$ core, and flat or specially certified curved Dirac convergence.

They are no longer the top-level hypothesis ledger.  In particular, the old
anchored fourth-order return identity is now only one sufficient clock
realization; the complete-cut exchange argument certifies freshness but not a
Levi--Civita connection; and the curved connection, ADM normalization, matter,
and continuum Einstein claims are superseded by the explicit source,
link/face, five-anomaly, and one-sequence theorems in the main body.  The legacy
route is retained to expose the exact logical ancestry and its countermodels.
\end{assessment}

\subsection{Exact scope of the word ``emergence''}

The theorem uses no prior manifold, metric tensor, causal cone, time coordinate,
spin structure, or spacetime dimension in the construction of the predictive
quotient, the signed cover, the projective revision algebra, or the renewal
pressure root.  The Lorentzian principal symbol and the limiting Dirac
Hamiltonian are reconstructed from finite predictive data.

For this certificate-level route, emergence means:
\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\text{finite predictive Store--Read data satisfying the explicit certificate}\\
\Longrightarrow\\
\text{Lorentzian Dirac geometry in a selected scaling phase.}
\end{gathered}}
\]
The master theorem above makes a smaller-source statement: the binary clock,
its complex factor, and its natural Reads are reconstructed from one anchored
return germ and the minimal future quotient.  Neither route claims that bare
composition alone forces spacetime or that all microscopic data are unique.

% =============================================================================
\section{Logical roles of the hypotheses}
% =============================================================================

\begin{definition}[Six logical roles]
Every premise in the theorem chain belongs to one of the following classes.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)},leftmargin=2.8em]
\item A \emph{foundational reconstruction condition} is used only when the
      finite complex operator description is itself reconstructed from a
      prelinear operational theory.
\item A \emph{microscopic model datum} specifies the concrete finite process:
      local Store coefficients, resolved branches, depths, capacities, record
      transports, and control operations.
\item A \emph{phase criterion} is a checkable property of the specified model,
      such as recurrent branching, coherent binary coupling, determinant-odd
      record transport, reversible accessibility, or exchange covariance.
\item An \emph{analytic scaling condition} controls a continuum limit, including
      finite range, uniform ellipticity, coefficient regularity, common cores,
      form convergence, and bounded subprincipal terms.
\item A \emph{physical selection} chooses one recurrent component, boundary
      condition, zero-source state, orientation sector, or scaling net when
      several admissible choices coexist.
\item A \emph{metrological calibration} converts dimensionless renewal depth
      into conventional time, length, or energy units.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Fit-like premise]
A premise is called \emph{fit-like} when its content is tailored mainly to force
an intended target---for example exactly one negative direction, exactly three
spatial directions, a round spatial metric, a positive clock exponent, or
vanishing torsion---rather than describing an independently motivated
microscopic phase or an ordinary analytic domain.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Restrictive is not synonymous with fit-like]
A phase criterion may be restrictive without being a numerical or structural
fit.  A coherent Store interaction, a noncycle recurrent graph, or a
common-source exchange law excludes consistent counterphases, but none of these
conditions explicitly inserts a Lorentzian metric or the number three.  The
fit-risk assessment in \Cref{sec:fit-risk} therefore records both the degree of
physical selectivity and how directly a premise resembles the desired
conclusion.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================================
\section{Primitive objects and the two entry routes}
% =============================================================================

\subsection{Operational primitive}

\begin{definition}[Finite operational process datum]
A finite operational process datum consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.6em]
\item a finite typed symmetric monoidal process category generated by finitely
      many preparations, transformations, resolved outcomes, swaps, identities,
      and discards;
\item convex mixing and real weights in $[0,1]$ for every closed experiment;
\item a specified family of admissible future continuations and terminal tests,
      closed under composition, classical postprocessing, and tensoring with
      identities;
\item a finite-dimensional reduced predictive sector after identifying all
      process differences invisible to the admitted experiments.
\end{enumerate}
A history is a generated composite.  The word ``future'' refers to admissible
postcomposition and does not presuppose physical time.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Future predictive quotient]
Two rooted histories $h,h'$ ending at the same operational type are future
equivalent when
\[
  \Pr(e\circ r\circ h)=\Pr(e\circ r\circ h')
\]
for every admissible continuation $r$ and terminal test $e$.  The quotient is
denoted $\Qq_+$.  Complete contextual equivalence is defined by the same rule
after adjoining arbitrary admitted ancillary systems and joint tests.
\end{definition}

\begin{assumption}[Operational reconstruction package]
\label{ass:operational-package}
When the complex operator description is reconstructed rather than directly
supplied, assume:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(O\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item The operational process datum is finite dimensional, convex, and reduced by complete contextual future equivalence. Every system possesses a unique deterministic discard effect, compatible with sequential and parallel composition.
\item pure processes are stable under admitted sequential and parallel
      composition, and every normalized state has a pure reversible dilation
      with the usual essential uniqueness on a minimal environment;
\item every reversible pure-state orbit contains one pure repeatable atomic
      observation branch, providing the preparation--observation sharp seed;
\item every nonclassical two-outcome face carries an effective transitive
      connected one-dimensional phase action with a consistent associative
      orientation across overlapping faces;
\item once the generated complex qubit block is present, ordinary physical
      monoidal closure under finite qubit-power ancillas is available;
\item if the complete composite operator theory is claimed, locally invisible
      holistic fibres are either absent or retained explicitly.  For the
      external Lorentzian calculus it is sufficient that all external
      Store--Read observables factor through the locally observable quotient.
\end{enumerate}
\end{assumption}

\subsection{Direct operator primitive}

\begin{definition}[Finite complete resolved renewal instrument]
A finite complete resolved renewal instrument is a tuple
\[
 \mathfrak R=
 \bigl(G,\{\A_x\}_{x\in V},\{\rho_x\}_{x\in V},
       \{\mathcal E_e\}_{e\in E_G},\ell,q,\mathscr H_{\Store}\bigr)
\]
with:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.6em]
\item a finite directed resolved graph $G$;
\item a finite-dimensional complex $C^*$-algebra $\A_x$ and a faithful
      normalized reference state $\rho_x$ at every reachable predictive vertex;
\item a CP trace-nonincreasing branch map
      $\mathcal E_e:\A_{s(e)}\to\A_{t(e)}$ for every resolved edge, with outgoing
      branches summing to a trace-preserving update;
\item positive finite depths $\ell_e$ and positive capacities $q_e$ on the
      supported edges;
\item an actual finite analytic probe-coupled Store Hamiltonian family
      $\mathscr H_{\Store}$ whose coefficient algebra determines the admitted
      local coherent controls and stable record algebra;
\item complete outcome resolution at the level of the physical pointer records
      used by the theorem.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Common finite object]
\label{prop:common-object}
Under \Cref{ass:operational-package}, the future quotient admits a finite complex
operator realization: a finite direct sum of matrix algebras acted on by a
finite alphabet of UCP predictive updates and resolved CP branches.  Conversely,
a finite complete resolved renewal instrument already supplies this object.
All Lorentzian theorems below begin from the common finite object and do not
otherwise depend on which entry route is used.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-common-object}.
\end{proof}

% =============================================================================
\section{Certificate-level finite and scaling ledger}
% =============================================================================

\begin{remark}[Legacy certificate rather than master axiom]
The clauses below are retained because the subsequent V053 proof chain is
self-contained in terms of them.  They are no longer the flagship entrance.
For a canonical anchored-clock cell, \Cref{thm:predictive-clock-discharge} and
\Cref{thm:complete-cut-factorization-master} derive the corresponding finite
structures from smaller tests.  For arbitrary models not yet connected to that
cell, the clauses remain independently checkable sufficient conditions.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Finite Store--Read Lorentzian phase certificate]
\label{ass:finite-phase}
Select one finite recurrent support and assume the following current
microscopic and phase conditions.

\paragraph{Recurrent renewal support.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(P\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item The supported resolved graph is strongly connected after restriction to
      the selected recurrent component, is not a single directed cycle, and has
      positive finite depths $0<\ell_{\min}\le\ell_e\le\ell_{\max}<\infty$.
\item Local Store actions and supported branch capacities have finite barriers
      on the selected compact control phase.  Consequently all supported
      equilibrium edges have positive weight and the relevant Perron transfer
      is irreducible.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Coherent Store seed and active closure.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(P\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em,start=3]
\item The actual analytic Store coefficient algebra has an isolated binary
      commutant closure: at the reference control value it resolves two pointer
      sectors, while on a punctured neighbourhood the common commutant on the
      active block is scalar.  Equivalently, some finite Taylor jet has a
      nonzero cross-support coefficient.
\item The KMS reverse of the active Store--Read calculus is retained; completed
      returns are one-leg local; and the retained diagonal controls act
      irreducibly on the active factor generated by the Store coefficients.
\item The interaction-complete monitoring and fresh-Read operations associated
      with the Store coefficient algebra are physically admitted.  Their stable
      pointer centre and its resolved branches are retained in the predictive
      record algebra.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Order-sensitive records and reversible accessibility.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(P\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em,start=6]
\item The two completed order records generated by the binary Store event are
      future distinct and survive the predictive quotient.  Their contrast
      transport is determinant regular, and at least one recurrent physical
      Read-leg-interchange loop acts by the transposition of the two records;
      equivalently its determinant holonomy is $-1$.
\item The active control orbit contains the predictive units generated by the
      Store closure.  After quotienting labels acting trivially, the realized
      nontrivial revision labels form a finite elementary abelian two-group
      $K_{\rev}$ whose composition law agrees with addition.  Each label is
      represented by a recurrent return and has a clock-preserving signed lift
      whose sheet shift is its determinant-holonomy pairing with $[\chi]$.
      Restricting to the generated simple block gives a faithful action.  After
      deriving the projective commutator form and quotienting its radical, the
      marked temporal label survives as a complement to the image $K$ of
      $K_{\rev}$: $H_{\rm prim}=K\oplus\F_2t$.  The determinant functional
      $k\mapsto\langle[\chi],k\rangle=\omega(t,k)$ is nonzero on $K$, and
      $K_0:=K\cap t^\perp$ is nonzero.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Spatial records and diamonds.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(P\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em,start=8]
\item The spectral half-records of the marked external Clifford frame are
      physically realized as resolved Read outcomes, or more generally the
      external direction record has a positive-definite second moment.
\item Every elementary two-direction diamond is generated from a common stored
      source followed by two fresh conditionally independent and identically
      distributed Reads.  More precisely, on its recurrent support there are a
      finite latent source set $Z$, a primitive source kernel $P$, and conditional
      Read laws $R_z$ such that
      \[
        K_\diamond((z,a,b),(z',a',b'))
        =P(z,z')R_{z'}(a')R_{z'}(b').
      \]
      The developed translational defect is odd under $(a,b)\leftrightarrow(b,a)$.
      No exchange condition is imposed on the rotational record.
\end{enumerate}
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[Continuum scaling package]
\label{ass:scaling-package}
For a scaling family indexed by $R\to\infty$, assume:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(A\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item the spatial mesh $h_R\downarrow0$, the reset stencil has uniformly finite
      range, and all matrix coefficients and bounded internal terms are
      uniformly controlled;
\item the external second-moment fields $M_R(x)$ converge locally uniformly to
      a uniformly positive field $M(x)\succeq m_0 I_3$;
\item in the flat theorem, $M$ is constant; in the curved theorem,
      $M\in C_b^4$, neighbouring record transports satisfy second-moment
      covariance $T_eM_xT_e^{\mathsf T}=M_y$ and preserve the selected
      determinant orientation, and the resulting discrete links are uniformly
      regular through the required order;
\item after the specified sampling/interpolation identification, all discrete
      Hamiltonians act on one Hilbert space and are self-adjoint on a common
      graph domain, or arise from closed forms with a common coercive bound;
      smooth compactly supported spinors lie in every domain and form a core for
      the limiting Hamiltonian;
\item the developed channel plaquettes admit, locally uniformly, the
      translational/rotational expansions
      \[
        \Pi_{\rm tr}\mathscr P_{ij}^{(h_R)}
        =h_R^2T_{ij}+O(h_R^3),
        \qquad
        \Pi_{\rm rot}\mathscr P_{ij}^{(h_R)}
        =-h_R^2F_{ij}+O(h_R^3),
      \]
      with the translational coefficient equal to the stationary developed
      defect; after the metric-compatible torsion-free connection is
      constructed, the discrete moment coefficients, their adjoints, the
      midpoint spin links, and the covariant differences satisfy the local
      consistency and compactness estimates needed through subprincipal order;
\item the residual scalar renewal term converges locally uniformly to a bounded
      real potential $V_{\rm ren}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[Selections and calibration]
\label{ass:selections}
The following are imposed only when the associated physical claim is made.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(S\arabic*)},leftmargin=3em]
\item Choose one recurrent component when several closed supports coexist.
\item Choose one deck-oriented extremal state, boundary condition, or
      superselection sector when a realized arrow of time is claimed.  The
      signed geometry and its two deck-related orientations exist without this
      state selection.
\item Choose the zero-source exchange phase when a torsion-free realized sector
      is claimed; other stationary phases may carry torsion.
\item Fix one conversion between renewal depth and conventional physical units.
      This sets the numerical values of the lapse and propagation speed in the
      chosen units but does not change the dimensionless theorem.
\end{enumerate}
\end{assumption}

\begin{remark}[What is not assumed]
The package does not postulate a Lorentzian metric, a causal cone, a Krein
fundamental symmetry, a Clifford algebra, a total factor of size four, a round
spatial covariance, a value of the pressure root, or a torsion-free connection.
Those objects appear as conclusions in the phase described above.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================================
\section{Certificate theorem I: predictive and signed finite geometry}
% =============================================================================

\begin{theorem}[Predictive reconstruction and positive geometry]
\label{thm:predictive-geometry}
Assume either the operational entry of \Cref{ass:operational-package} or the
direct resolved instrument entry.  Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item future equivalence produces the unique reduced future-sufficient memory;
\item resolved-outcome conditioning gives a well-defined successor map, and in
      every reduced future-tomographic realization
      \[
        \mathcal E_e(\rho_x)=w_e\rho_{t(e)},
        \qquad
        w_e=\Tr\mathcal E_e(\rho_x),
      \]
      so resolved path weights multiply;
\item the accumulated UCP maps form a predictive monoid and every unit of that
      monoid is a $*$-automorphism;
\item if the descended predictive shifts have uniformly finite fibres and
      bounded quotient-length increments, the quotient length defines a
      positive spectral triple whose zeta abscissa is the predictive growth
      exponent;
\item divisibility in the predictive monoid, after quotienting mutual
      reachability, defines a partial order and an intrinsic increasing renewal
      time function on every regular graded component;
\item the irreducible positive sector cannot functorially produce a nonconstant
      fundamental symmetry.  Every compatible nonconstant diagonal involution
      contains additional $\Ztwo$ cocycle data.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-predictive-geometry}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Minimal signed cover and modular clock]
\label{thm:signed-clock}
Under \Cref{ass:finite-phase}\textup{(P1)--(P6)}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item the determinant signs of the future-distinct record contrast define a
      nontrivial class
      \[
        [\chi]\in H^1(G;\Ztwo);
      \]
\item the objectwise-minimal cone-preserving fibrewise-indefinite enrichment is
      the principal double cover $G^\chi\to G$, with fibre rank two and
      fundamental symmetry
      \[
        J_\chi e_{(x,\eta)}=\eta e_{(x,\eta)};
      \]
\item a finite amplitude square-root lift exists in $H^1(G;\Zfour)$, but the
      real Lorentzian conclusions depend only on $[\chi]$;
\item the unweighted depth transfer
      \[
        B(s)_{xy}=\sum_{e:x\to y}e^{-s\ell_e}
      \]
      has a unique positive root $\beta_{\rm top}$ satisfying
      $\rho(B(\beta_{\rm top}))=1$;
\item for any positive weighted transfer in the same finite-depth phase, a
      positive root $\beta$ is unique whenever its zero-depth Perron radius is
      greater than one;
\item on the graded signed path cover, let $N$ be accumulated renewal
      depth, so an edge $e$ raises $N$ by $\ell_e$.  Within the natural
      atomic-diagonal class, let $D_0$ be an invertible self-adjoint deck-odd
      unbounded operator whose positive magnitude implements the
      depth-homogeneous pressure character,
      \[
        \Ad(|D_0|)(\widetilde S_e)
        =e^{\beta\ell_e}\widetilde S_e.
      \]
      Then, after a flat sheet gauge,
      \[
        D_0=J_\chi e^{\beta N}
      \]
      up to one overall positive magnitude normalization.  A bounded
      $J_\chi$-self-adjoint finite-propagation term carries the remaining
      differential content.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-signed-clock}.
\end{proof}

% =============================================================================
\section{Certificate theorem II: the finite external Lorentzian core}
% =============================================================================

\begin{theorem}[Automatic projective revision algebra]
\label{thm:projective-revision}
Under \Cref{ass:finite-phase}\textup{(P3)--(P7)}, let $K_{\rev}$ be the realized
elementary-two revision group on the selected active matrix factor.  Choose
unitary implementers $R_\alpha$ of its automorphisms.  Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item there is a normalized scalar cocycle $\sigma$ such that
      \[
        R_\alpha R_\beta
        =\sigma(\alpha,\beta)R_{\alpha+\beta};
      \]
\item its antisymmetric ratio defines an intrinsic alternating form
      \[
        \sigma(\alpha,\beta)\sigma(\beta,\alpha)^{-1}
        =(-1)^{\omega_{\spat}(\alpha,\beta)},
        \qquad
        \omega_{\spat}\in\Alt^2(K_{\rev},\F_2);
      \]
\item adjoining the temporal label $t$ represented by $J_\chi$ gives the full
      form
      \[
      \omega((\alpha,\epsilon),(\beta,\delta))
      =\omega_{\spat}(\alpha,\beta)
       +\epsilon\langle[\chi],\beta\rangle
       +\delta\langle[\chi],\alpha\rangle;
      \]
\item quotienting $\rad\omega$ gives a nondegenerate finite symplectic module
      $H_{\rm prim}$ and a canonical primitive matrix type
      \[
        \C_\sigma[H_{\rm prim}]
        \cong M_{2^m}(\C)
        \cong\Cl_{2m}(\C),
        \qquad \dim_{\F_2}H_{\rm prim}=2m.
      \]
\end{enumerate}
The cocycle, alternating form, radical, and primitive Clifford type are
consequences of the reversible action; they are not separately specified data.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-projective-revision}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Minimal external $M_4(\C)$ core]
\label{thm:external-core}
Under \Cref{ass:finite-phase}\textup{(P3)--(P8)}, write the marked
nondegenerate primitive module as
\[
  H_{\rm prim}=K\oplus\F_2t,
  \qquad K_0:=K\cap t^\perp\ne0.
\]
There is a unique nonzero $r\in\rad(\omega|_K)$, it satisfies
$\omega(t,r)=1$, and $K_0$ is nondegenerate.  Choosing any nonzero
$b\in K_0$ and $c\in K_0$ with $\omega(b,c)=1$ gives an orthogonal
symplectic decomposition
\[
  H_{\rm prim}=W_{\ext}\perp H_{\intr},
  \qquad W_{\ext}=\Span\{t,r,b,c\},
  \qquad \dim_{\F_2}W_{\ext}=4,
\]
with corresponding factorization
\[
  \boxed{
  \A_{\rm act}
  \cong
  \underbrace{M_4(\C)}_{\A_{\ext}\cong\Cl_4(\C)}
  \otimes
  \underbrace{M_{2^{m-2}}(\C)}_{\A_{\intr}}.}
\]
The external factor contains:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item a Hermitian grading generator $J_\chi$;
\item a realized revision $R_{\alpha_0}$ with
      $J_\chi R_{\alpha_0}=-R_{\alpha_0}J_\chi$;
\item the anti-Hermitian temporal Clifford generator
      \[
        \gamma^0=J_\chi R_{\alpha_0},
        \qquad (\gamma^0)^2=-\one;
      \]
\item three Hermitian spatial Clifford generators
      $\gamma^1,\gamma^2,\gamma^3$.
\end{enumerate}
Thus $(3+1)$ is the minimal spatially nondegenerate external Lorentzian core.
The theorem does not require $H_{\intr}=0$ and therefore does not assert
uniqueness of the total primitive dimension.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-external-core}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Canonical external direction record]
\label{thm:octahedral-record}
On the marked external factor, let
\[
  P_{i,\pm}=\frac12(\one\pm\gamma^i),
  \qquad i=1,2,3,
\]
and define
\[
  E_{i,\pm}=\frac13P_{i,\pm},
  \qquad K_{i,\pm}=\frac1{\sqrt3}P_{i,\pm},
  \qquad
  \mathcal I_{i,\pm}(\rho)=K_{i,\pm}\rho K_{i,\pm}^*.
\]
Whenever these spectral Read outcomes are physically admitted,
$\{\mathcal I_{i,\pm}\}$ is a trace-preserving resolved Lueders instrument
with direction labels $\theta_{i,\pm}=\pm e_i\in\Sphere^2$.  For every external density matrix $\rho$,
\[
  \sum_{i,\pm}\Tr(\rho E_{i,\pm})\,
       \theta_{i,\pm}\theta_{i,\pm}^{\mathsf T}
  =\frac13 I_3.
\]
Consequently the external spatial second moment is positive definite and
state independent, and with frame normalization $\kappa_{\rm frame}=3$ the
finite resolved record gives the exact principal symbol
\[
  i\left(\gamma^0\xi_0+\sum_{i=1}^3\gamma^i\xi_i\right),
\]
whose square is
\[
  (\xi_0^2-|\vec\xi|^2)\one.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-octahedral-record}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[General anisotropic phase]
The continuum theorem does not require the octahedral realization.  Any
positive-definite external second moment $M$ defines a spatial vielbein and is
constant-frame equivalent to the standard flat Dirac Hamiltonian.  The
spectral instrument above gives a particularly canonical finite representative
of the external frame when it is physically available.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================================
\section{Certificate theorem III: flat Lorentzian Dirac continuum}
% =============================================================================

\begin{definition}[External signed principal symbol]
For a symmetric positive-definite external second moment
$M\in\operatorname{Sym}_3(\R)$ and $\kappa>0$, define
\[
 \sigma_M(\xi)
 :=i\left(\gamma^0\xi_0+
          \kappa\sum_{i,j=1}^3\gamma^iM^{ij}\xi_j\right).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Lorentzian symbol and flat Hamiltonian limit]
\label{thm:flat-limit}
Assume \Cref{ass:finite-phase}\textup{(P1)--(P8)} and the flat specialization of
\Cref{ass:scaling-package}.  Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item the principal symbol obeys
      \[
        \boxed{
        \sigma_M(\xi)^2
        =\left(\xi_0^2-
          \kappa^2\vec\xi^{\,\mathsf T}M^2\vec\xi\right)\one,}
      \]
      so $M\succ0$ gives signature $(1,3)$;
\item constant anisotropy is an external frame choice: after the spatial linear
      change $y=\kappa^{-1}M^{-1}x$, the limiting Hamiltonian is unitarily
      equivalent to the standard massless $(3+1)$ Dirac Hamiltonian;
\item the covariant discrete spacetime Dirac operators converge on a common
      smooth core to the continuum first-order operator;
\item the associated self-adjoint spatial Hamiltonians $\Ham_R$ converge in
      strong-resolvent sense to
      \[
        \Ham_M=\kappa\sum_{i,j=1}^3
        (\gamma^0\gamma^i)M^{ij}(-i\partial_j);
      \]
\item the propagators converge strongly, uniformly for time in compact
      intervals, and $f(\Ham_R)\to f(\Ham_M)$ strongly for every
      $f\in C_0(\R)$;
\item on every fixed compact momentum band, the symmetric finite-difference
      scheme converges in norm resolvent, with second-order consistency for an
      exact centered frame.
\end{enumerate}
No full norm-resolvent statement is asserted for the unmodified symmetric mesh
on the entire momentum space.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-flat-limit}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Self-averaged flat endpoint]
\label{cor:self-averaged-flat}
Let $(\theta_a)_{a\ge1}$ be a stationary ergodic sequence of external reset
directions with marginal law $\zeta_*$ and second moment
\[
  M_*:=\int_{\Sphere^2}\theta\theta^{\mathsf T}\,\dd\zeta_*(\theta)\succ0.
\]
For any $m_R\to\infty$ and $h_R\downarrow0$, form the empirical direction law
from $\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_{m_R}$ and the corresponding centered-difference
Hamiltonian.  Then, almost surely, the empirical second moments converge to
$M_*$ and the random discrete Hamiltonians converge in strong-resolvent sense
to the deterministic flat Lorentzian Hamiltonian $\Ham_{M_*}$.  Rotational
invariance of the microscopic direction law is unnecessary.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-self-averaging}.
\end{proof}

\section{Certificate theorem IV: curved Levi--Civita continuum}
% =============================================================================

\begin{definition}[Renewal coframe and metric]
For a smooth uniformly positive moment field $M(x)$, define
\[
  e_a{}^j(x)=\kappa M^{aj}(x),
  \qquad
  g^{-1}(x)=\diag\bigl(1,-\kappa^2M(x)^2\bigr).
\]
The normalized comparison of neighboring moment frames is the orthogonal part
of their polar decomposition.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exchange-selected stationary odd-defect cancellation]
\label{thm:torsion-cancellation}
Under \Cref{ass:finite-phase}\textup{(P9)}, let $\pi_Z$ be the unique
stationary law of the latent source kernel $P$ and let $\widetilde\pi_{ij}$ be
the stationary law of the elementary common-source diamond kernel.  Then
\[
 \widetilde\pi_{ij}(z,a,b)
 =\pi_Z(z)R_z(a)R_z(b)
 =\widetilde\pi_{ij}(z,b,a).
\]
If $d^{\rm tr}_{ij}(z,b,a)=-d^{\rm tr}_{ij}(z,a,b)$, then
\[
  \sum_{z,a,b}\widetilde\pi_{ij}(z,a,b)
  d^{\rm tr}_{ij}(z,a,b)=0.
\]
The rotational plaquette record is not constrained by this cancellation and
may have nonzero holonomy.  This theorem proves only the vanishing of the
stationary mean of the specified exchange-odd translational observable.  It
does not prove pointwise discrete torsion, metric compatibility, orientation,
or same-history face composition; those are decided by
\Cref{thm:physical-connection-master}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-torsion-cancellation}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Legacy curved Levi--Civita strong-resolvent limit]
\label{thm:curved-limit}
Assume \Cref{ass:finite-phase}, \Cref{ass:scaling-package}, and the zero-source
selection of \Cref{ass:selections}\textup{(S3)}.  In addition, assume that the
actual two-cell link and same-history face data pass the Levi--Civita branch
\textup{(L1)} of \Cref{thm:physical-connection-master}.  Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item the physical link certificate supplies frame closure, metric
      compatibility, orientability, and same-history plaquette composition;
\item the explicit discrete torsion certificate vanishes; the stationary
      cancellation in \Cref{thm:torsion-cancellation} is only a consistency
      check on its exchange-odd mean;
\item the Levi--Civita branch determines the discrete spin connection, while
      rotational plaquette holonomy remains available as curvature;
\item the covariant renewal Hamiltonians $\Ham_R^{\LC}$ converge in
      strong-resolvent sense to
      \[
        \Ham_g^{\LC}+V_{\rm ren};
      \]
\item the propagators converge strongly and uniformly on compact time
      intervals, and smooth compactly supported spinors have the stated
      subprincipal consistency;
\item the limiting operator depends on the microscopic direction law only
      through the renormalized moment field $M(x)$, the external Clifford datum,
      the clock normalization, and the bounded scalar renewal potential.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
See the detailed proof in \Cref{app:proof-curved-limit}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Curvature is not suppressed]
\label{cor:curvature-survives}
The torsion-free conclusion does not imply flatness.  The translational defect
and rotational record belong to distinct plaquette components.  Exchange
symmetry cancels the translational component and leaves the rotational component unconstrained, so the limiting
Levi--Civita connection may have nonzero curvature.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
See \Cref{app:proof-curvature-survives}.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Deepest certificate-level finite condition]
Within the self-contained certificate route, the most selective finite input
is the combined claim that one recurrent binary Store event is coherently
noncommutative, that its completed order records are retained, and that its
active control orbit is reversibly accessible.  At the master level this is
not imposed clause by clause for the canonical cell: it is discharged by the
anchor-return rigidity, depth-two Clifford, and complete-cut theorems.  It
remains a useful diagnostic for concrete processes not yet shown to belong to
that class.
\end{assessment}

\begin{assessment}[Second major residual]
A realized arrow of time is not selected by the finite signed geometry itself.
The double cover determines two deck-related orientations and an intrinsic
odd observable, but a finite primitive deck-symmetric stationary law is
orientation symmetric.  The choice of one temporal orientation is therefore an
additional physical state selection.
\end{assessment}

\begin{assessment}[Geometric model dependence]
The theorem determines how a positive second-moment field becomes a Lorentzian
metric and how exchange closure becomes Levi--Civita transport.  It does not
determine a unique field $M(x)$ from no model data.  The particular curved
spacetime is selected by the microscopic renewal statistics, boundary data,
and scaling phase.
\end{assessment}

\section{Precise nonclaims and open boundaries}
% =============================================================================

\begin{proposition}[No-overstatement boundary]
\label{prop:no-overstatement}
The present flagship theorem does not imply any of the following.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)},leftmargin=2.7em]
\item Every finite operational or quantum process becomes Lorentzian spacetime.
\item The total primitive factor is uniquely $M_4(\C)$.
\item The predictive spectral growth dimension is automatically four.
\item A finite direction record produces convergence of the discrete order cone
      to the smooth Lorentzian causal cone without an additional density or
      refinement theorem.
\item A nontrivial signed cover dynamically chooses one orientation in a finite
      symmetric stationary phase.
\item Stationarity, reversibility, or detailed balance alone removes torsion.
\item The symmetric lattice Hamiltonian converges in full norm resolvent on the
      entire momentum space.
\item The limiting metric profile or global topology is uniquely fixed by the
      primitive operational conditions.
\item The Levi--Civita curvature satisfies Einstein's equations or any other
      gravitational field equation.
\item Matter multiplets, gauge interactions, quantum measurement, black-hole
      thermodynamics, or cosmological dynamics follow from the kinematic theorem.
\item The dimensionless pressure clock fixes an absolute number of seconds or
      an absolute energy without calibration.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
See the countermodel and independence proof in \Cref{app:proof-nonclaims}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Order cone and Dirac cone]
The characteristic cone of the signed Dirac symbol is quadratic and Lorentzian.
A finite deterministic predictive divisibility cone can remain polyhedral.
Equating these two notions requires a separate continuum comparison of the
order structure, not merely convergence of the Hamiltonian symbol.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Predictive dimension and Clifford dimension]
The growth exponent of distinguishable accumulated channels and the rank of the
external Clifford factor measure different structures.  Their equality in a
regular spacetime-crystal phase is an additional conclusion under corresponding
growth and coding hypotheses, not part of the core Lorentzian emergence theorem.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Continuous Hamiltonian time]
The continuum operator theorem uses a self-adjoint Hamiltonian and a continuous
evolution parameter.  It does not construct a fully discrete unitary evolution
with the timelike direction itself placed on the same spatial mesh.  Such a
construction requires a separate stability or CFL theorem.
\end{remark}
